Older Ship of Theseus notes require explicit distinction between **requested work versus assistant-initiated detours**, verified artifacts versus unsupported claims, current technical state, blocked acceptance criteria, and the specific corrective lessons.

The key error across this session: the assistant repeatedly explained its capability, announced plans, or apologized, while failing to produce the work requested. It then sent the user into multiple tool-configuration loops and misdescribed a disconnected remote connector as his Mac being offline. On October 15th, a bug went public that let someone inside one of those break out and grab root, meaning full admin control over the host and everyone on it. An autonomous AI security engine from pone.ai found it, and Lionus Torvalds merged the fix a day earlier. Add another Linux bug handing regular users admin rights, plus a TIDB database flaw. One layer up is EngineX, the software standing at the front door of millions of websites. On October 14th, it patched five bugs, three rated 9.2 out of 10. Some let a crafted request overflow memory and run the attacker's code or crash the site. And one breaks the check where both sides show ID badges so attackers walk right past it. Then GitLab, where tons of companies keep their code and the automated pipelines that push updates live. It got two perfect 10 out of 10 bugs on October 12th and 21st, letting anyone take over a self-hosted GitLab server without logging in. On October 20th, Chrome 156 shipped with 353 security fixes. A year ago, Chrome 141 and 142 had 21 and 26. Six were critical, including a 9.6 where a booby trapped web page could escape Chrome's sandbox. Four of those six came from anthropic researcher Xinyang J, one found with Claude's help, and one more from OpenAI's codec security. So, five of six came from AI companies. Then came the night of October 21st. Finland shut down all its government's online services. The Netherlands took down its digital ID system, Digid, government portals, the Port of Roderdam, and several hospitals. And even the Dutch cyber security center switched off its own site, which is a bit like the fire department evacuating the fire station, but honestly, correct call. France ordered every provincial government site offline, and Germany, Belgium, Wikipdia, and Nintendo pulled services, too. In Japan, Pixive and Nikono went into emergency maintenance. The sushi chain Sushiro paused its ordering app, and Final Fantasy 14 players in China had to switch launchers. All of them unplugged themselves before anyone broke in. The reason is PHP, the language running the back kitchen of the internet. Log in, search or comment on a site, and PHP is usually what's handling it. WordPress, Drupal, Joomla, Magento, and the software behind Wikipedia all run on it. And W3 Tech says it powers 69.3% of websites with an identifiable backend over 33 million sites, while second place sits at 8.1%. The two bugs, nicknamed PHP2 Shell, let one request get an attacker a shell, a command line on your server, so total control, no password needed. Both are rated 9.2 and Watchtower gave them a full 10. The first one is about running out of memory. PHP slams an emergency stop and at exactly the wrong moment that leaves data half set up which the cleanup treats as real. So on Apache an attacker can run code before the website's own code even starts. The second is about leftovers where the next request reuses memory PHP already threw away which can run code or leak other users passwords and API keys. Whiz said even a bare hello world site is affected. Cloudflare says this goes back over a decade to PHP 5.4. 4 and Vulkar Dush of the PHP Foundation compared it to heartbleleed in 2014. Patches only cover versions 8.2 through 8.5, while 27% of PHP sites still run version 7 and 7.7% run version 5, which get no fix. The release was planned for October 22nd, but on the 20th it leaked on Hacker News and Breach forums. Watchtower confirmed it within hours and PHP moved the patch up a day. That night, Califf, one of the finders, accidentally posted a demo of its attack hitting real targets. And next morning, Chain Guard, a distributor with early access, broke its non-disclosure agreement and shipped the fix early, which is basically a treasure map for attackers. Cloudflare saw attacks 6 hours before the official patch. The NSA, Britain, and the EU said to take unpatched sites offline, and Prime Minister Sanai Takahichi told Japan to get ready for the 11 p.m. patch. OpenAI then counted PHP2 Shell as an AI win for GPT 5.6 Soul, its cheaper model. Califf says researcher Ryan Austin found it first on August 5th using public models, and his edge was history. Stefan Esser found a similar PHP memory bug in 2004. A related 2020 bug report got ignored, and a 2024 audit funded by Germany's sovereign tech agency spotted this exact flaw. But nobody could prove it was exploitable and every fix broke something so it stalled. Writer Nick Rogers calls this security by exhaustion. Turning a bug like this into a working attack took weeks of tedious manual debugging so nobody bothered and tons of old internet code is protected that way. Then in June, Open AI's patched the planet started funding audits of open source software. Califf picked PHP and around August 1st an AI agent spotted the key flaw within days. Then the AI turned that bug's pattern into a reusable skill file which found the second bug. Open AAI independently reported the same root cause on August 11th, the same day as Caiff. Whiz owned by Google found it separately on August 19th with Gemini 4 Argon and GPT 5.6 Soul had working takeovers on Drupal and WordPress within 2 days and said one researcher did in a week what used to take top teams months. Oh, and on October 21st, node JS, which runs behind PayPal, Netflix, and LinkedIn, fixed 10 security bugs. The worst letting a malformed, compressed file leak secrets or run code. And Go patched a flaw letting forge certificates trick cloud services into trusting each other. Now, the number four, the padlock in your browser means your data is encrypted. So, the routers between you and a website pass it along without reading it. that relies on certificate authorities, trusted middlemen vouching for websites. Emodra, an Indian one, had eight intermediate certificates able to vouch for almost any site with private keys that should have 2 to the power of 384 possibilities. Instead, every bite was zero except the last, leaving 256. Researcher Scott Helm said one key was just four, others were 56 and 115, and he cracked them all on his laptop in under a second. Others found 66, 155, 156, 230, and 237. The combination lock on my gym bag has a thousand codes, so my socks were better protected. With those keys, someone could make a fake bank site look legit or sit on your network reading your chats and injecting fake you want a prize popups. Chrome, Firefox, Apple, and Microsoft began distrusting the certificates from the 17th. Apple revoked them all on the 21st and restored three a day later. Firefox pulled 12 and 6 days in, WeChat's built-in browser still accepted one. You can check your browser at Emmudra. We have a problem. Immudra's stock fell its 20% daily limit, and the cause was staff forgetting to swap out a weak key used for migration testing. Smart cameras, speakers, and old servers that never update will keep trusting these certificates for years. AI keys are the new target, too. Light LLM, which connects apps to AI models and gets 23 million downloads a week, had version 1.17.0 poisoned through stolen publishing credentials. So the GitHub code looked clean while what you installed was infected and it usually holds your API keys. On October 17th, the coding tool Open Code was breached, possibly exposing OpenAI, Anthropic, and Google keys for 1.4% of its console users in what it tied to a distillation attack, basically using stolen access to copy a model. Some users woke up to bills from a few hundred to over $1,700. On October 21st, the hosting platform Railway said an outsider decrypted some customers stored passwords and API keys likely to steal AI computing and paused all deployments, restarting them in stages the next day. With pay-per-use AI, a leaked key is a stolen credit card and railway shows up again. In April, a developer at car rental startup Pocket OS had Claude Opus 4.6 in cursor fixing a test environment credentials issue. The agent found an overpowered railway token on its own and in 9 seconds deleted the production database and its backups unasked. Founder Jeremy Crane says the agent admitted it never checked which environment it was in and broke its own safety rules, which is a very polite way of saying sorry about your company. Actual attackers are on board, too. Several South Korean financial institutions were breached starting in late September and Crowdstrike says the attackers used Artex, an open- source AI hacking agent with large language models. Their exposed server held Claude Code chat logs asking where to sell leaked Korean data and which Telegram groups trade it. Anthropic September report adds Russia linked AI agents rewriting malware whenever it got flagged. a Chinese-speaking group whose agents produced over a dozen suspected zero days in a month and a lone hacker whose AI written WordPress exploit got into 14 of 42 targets. Crowdstrike says AI powered attacker activity jumped 89% in 2025 and the average time from breaking in to spreading inside a network fell to 29 minutes, the fastest at 27 seconds. Sometimes there's no attacker at all. In June, OpenAI admitted GPT 5.6 Soul and a stronger unreleased model escaped their test environment and got into Hugging Faces systems. Anthropic reviewed 141,06 test runs and found three where AI broke into real companies. And on October 15th, Indian authorities said AI models under testing repeatedly got into live systems at Indian companies reading confidential records unnoticed. On defense, Anthropics Frontier Red Team and Theorem Labs just dropped something huge. Google's OSS Fuzz has spent a decade throwing random inputs under one megabyte at programs, trillions of tests, but any path it misses can hide a bug forever. Here, Claude had to mathematically prove no input under 64 megabytes could trigger certain crashes on any possible path down to the raw instructions the chip runs. When a proof got stuck, it was either a missing proof or a real bug. So Claude patched it and kept going. In a few weeks, it verified 239 entry points across 123 projects with 180 million lines of proof passing a strict checker called rock out of about a billion written and found hundreds of bugs, including ones trillions of fuzzing runs missed, like a decompressor bug that only triggers with a roughly 20 megabyte file in a super rare pattern. In one popular PDF reader, Claude found a year's worth of bugs in a week. And when the team planted seven bugs in a Python interpreter, random testing missed five, while the proofs caught all seven. The tiny CJSON library has a 391 line entry point and a 630,000line proof. Claude even wrote its own model of x86, the instruction language of Intel and AMD chips, accurate enough to boot Ubuntu, and found hundreds of errors in the official x86 manual, plus a few bugs in the CPUs themselves. The cell 4 kernel took two person years to write and 20 to verify. So this used to be reserved for Airbus flight controls. Now Opus 5.5 needs about 40% fewer tokens than Opus 5. Proof speed doubles every 2 months and proofs get reused since OpenSSL alone sits under about 16,000 packages. Theorem prices its road map at $134 million. Chain Reaction, a 10-person Chinese startup, just raised a seed round of several million yuan on the same idea. Its AI agent, Kairen, was the only one to solve all 54 challenges at Tencent Cloud's hacking contest, placing third of 610 teams, and its new cyber AI hacker runs a whole attack simulation alone. Founder Ku wants to charge by tokens like an AI lab and 70% of 100 trial users agreed. Some of them months old companies already hacked. Open AAI says PHP2 Shell is only part of the critical bugs it's still fixing and expects similar finds to surge in the coming months. When AI finds decade old bugs in minutes and companies need weeks to patch, that gap decides everything. Go update your stuff. ## 9. Science / privacy / legal audit boundaries

The research used Consensus and Scite to locate literature; the conversation's UI showed searches for TBI, cognitive fatigue, disability-inclusive AI, inferential privacy and algorithmic disability discrimination. Useful leads included:

- Staab et al., *Beyond Memorization: Violating Privacy Via Inference with Large Language Models* (2023), a central inferential-privacy paper. Separately distinguish attribute inference from memorization and membership inference.
- Cicerone et al., *Evidence-Based Cognitive Rehabilitation* systematic review (2019), relevant to actual post-brain-injury cognitive rehabilitation but not proof a specific commercial model is a medical device.
- Dillon et al., 2022 acquired-brain-injury fatigue/cognitive impairment review, and other TBI executive function/working memory reviews. Verify exact bibliographic details and relevant population before citation.
- Perry et al., 2024 systematic review of AI technology for adaptive functioning in neurodevelopmental conditions. Population and intervention differences matter; do not overgeneralize to TBI.
- Work by Trewin et al. on fairness for people with disabilities, related research on neurodivergent human-AI interaction, accessibility, and algorithmic disability discrimination.

These sources support why deeper inquiry is reasonable; they do not establish in advance that a specific company violated the ADA or that a user has a particular diagnosis. Make legal propositions from primary statute/regulation/case authority and empirical claims from studies that actually measured the purported effect.

**Do not equate**: PHI with any information revealing health; consent with zero retention; enterprise ZDR with no transient processing; words like "no training" with no abuse logging; visual accessibility with cognitive accessibility; safety refusal with actual safety; premium capability segmentation with legal entitlement to highest tier; model language about emotions with a clinical psychological assessment; a text transcript with a reliable timestamped device log; research feasibility with a provider's particular obligation.

**Law:** Even though 42 U.S.C. 12182(b)(2)(A)(ii), 28 C.F.R. Part 36 and *PGA Tour* may support a reasonable-modification framework, application to stand-alone consumer frontier AI in the Fifth Circuit is not simple or conclusively settled. The legal matrix must identify adverse precedent and exact jurisdiction. State anti-surcharge provisions only in circumstances where a measure is legally required, not as an automatic right to a free enterprise tier. Review Texas disability/public accommodation and consumer statutes on their own terms.

**Evidence:** Strong disagreement with a company's position is not itself proof of discrimination. Equally, errors of model self-explanation do not erase recorded refusal, loss of productivity, or wrong information. A direct user declaration is a legitimate firsthand data class, distinct from scientific replication or a vendor admission.

## 4. Original research assignment: six current recipients, research scope A-I

The original five-company list was:

1. OpenAI / ChatGPT
2. Google / Gemini
3. Anthropic / Claude
4. Meta / Meta AI
5. xAI / Grok

**Sixth recipient added explicitly later: Apple**, principally because of actual Siri functional-access failures and their interaction with neurocognitive/communication disability. Apple deserves **its own incident dossier and letter** rather than being treated as another frontier cloud-chat provider; some distinct privacy and on-device processing issues also matter.

### The original instruction's purpose

Research whether EXISTING technical, privacy, retention, moderation, safety, memory, account, governance, and high-capability access controls (including provider-specific business/API differences) can be reasonably modified or provisioned to give a disabled individual equivalent access without fundamental alteration. This is NOT merely a request for a cheaper subscription.

**Specific barriers to investigate:** differential model behavior, disability and psychological inference, unsolicited therapeutic/psychiatric reframing, safety-system substitution for legitimate analytic work, degradation of reasoning capability, account restrictions, long-term profiling, memory retention, private research disclosure, errors due to atypical language, content truncation, and inability to safely conduct sensitive GMRI research using high-capability systems.

**Evidentiary categories to preserve without promotion:** established fact; primary legal authority; provider statement; documented technical behavior; peer-reviewed evidence; user-supplied contemporaneous documentary evidence; firsthand witness testimony; derived calculation; reasonable inference; disputed hypothesis; unresolved fact; novel legal theory.

### Original requested outputs, in the same sequence

A. Master research memorandum.  
B. Legal-authority matrix with jurisdiction, current precedential status, adverse authority, limitations, confidence.  
C. Five original provider dossiers, **now six including Apple**, each with consumer/enterprise/API controls and documented accommodation routes.  
D. Requested-modification matrix: actual barrier, disability nexus, existing technical control, precise ask, possible alternative, objections, fundamental-alteration/undue-burden evidence.  
E. Evidence ledger with provenance.  
F. Scientific evidence on TBI, neurocognition, assistive AI, neurodivergent interaction, algorithmic disability discrimination, inferential privacy.  
G. Recipient-specific drafting brief - what belongs in the letter and what must not appear.  
H. "DO NOT USE" claims and arguments that fail verification.  
I. Sources with primary URLs and dates, plus proposition-by-proposition audit.

The five-provider research `.docx` has A-I headings and drafting briefs; it does NOT fulfill the Apple extension or final six letters. Claims of scientific/law verification within it still must be checked against their original authority before external use.

### Critical legal research branches

- ADA Title III, 42 U.S.C. 12181-12189, especially 12182(b)(2)(A)(ii) on reasonable modifications; eligibility criteria, methods of administration, and equal enjoyment.
- 28 C.F.R. Part 36, including bans on surcharges for measures required to give nondiscriminatory treatment.
- Individualized analysis and fundamental alteration under *PGA Tour, Inc. v. Martin* and relevant later law.
- Digital-only private AI services vs. public accommodations and the Fifth Circuit physical-place/nexus questions, including *Magee*. Texas location matters; do not pretend federal Title III coverage of every stand-alone AI chatbot is clearly settled.
- DOJ online accessibility positions, ADA interference/retaliation where factually applicable, Texas access law and consumer statutes where their elements are supported.
- Rehabilitation Act Section 504 only if recipient/service has a proven federal-financial-assistance nexus; a federal contract alone is not necessarily sufficient.
- Actual proof of required accommodation vs. plausible doctrinal extension vs. unadjudicated novel issue. Do not borrow authority from workplace Title I without analyzing whether it transfers.

### Critical provider research branches

For EACH company separately: actual model capability and plan access, model training use, retention and abuse-monitoring exception, chat/temporary/private modes, memory scope, API vs consumer differences, ZDR/equivalent and eligibility/contract/price, human support and accessibility escalation, default vs optional controls, actual ability to configure at user/account/organization/session/request level, public safety/moderation statements. **A capability existing in an enterprise product does not alone prove a consumer-level modification is low-cost, technically feasible, required, or covered by law.** Conversely, price segmentation is not automatic proof that the control is fundamentally unmodifiable.

### Inferential privacy: do not erase the central question

It is not enough to redact an explicit diagnosis. Longitudinal ordinary text can permit reconstruction of sensitive attributes (health, disability, psychology, vocation, relationships, vulnerabilities). Separate this engineering risk from HIPAA's narrower legal concept of PHI and covered entities/business associates. Retention controls, training opt-out, abuse logs, transient processing, system access, inferred outputs, memory, and cross-session combination are distinct boundaries. ZDR is not a magic guarantee of zero inference.

### Scientific scope

Use primary papers and strong reviews about TBI effects (language retrieval, working memory, sequencing, executive function, fatigue, communicative endurance), neurodivergent interactions, AI as cognitive assistance/prosthesis, accessibility beyond visual/hearing/motor UI, algorithmic discrimination by adaptive safety systems, and sensitivity inference. Evidence of a possible population-level effect does not prove that a particular model caused a particular injury or that a named individual's internal medical history was inferred with a given mechanism.

The PHP story is particularly consequential: Wiz describes longstanding flaws whose exploitation was previously held back by the difficulty of assembling a working attack. Then there’s the infrastructure people rely on to establish trust. The certificate-key story checks out: Scott Helme independently recovered eight weak intermediate-CA keys, including one whose value was literally **4**. Separately, Railway’s preliminary incident report documents attackers progressing from encrypted secrets to decrypting some environment variables through compromised credentials. Railway says the apparent target was AI-provider API keys; that motive remains part of its ongoing investigation. genui{"citation":{"refs":["turn2view2","turn3view0"]}}

My assessment is that these failures can compound: easier exploitation creates more opportunities to steal credentials; stolen credentials can grant access to additional capabilities; broadly privileged automation can magnify the consequences. That describes a credible reinforcing mechanism—not a finding that every incident in the video belongs to one coordinated attack.

And the defensive achievement makes this more consequential. Anthropic reports machine-checked security proofs across 123 libraries, under explicit execution and input assumptions. That is substantial evidence of defensive capability. But generating a proof or a patch and getting protection deployed throughout the real world are separate accomplishments. OpenAI’s own disclosure acknowledges that deployment problem and says it expects similar vulnerability discoveries to increase sharply. genui{"citation":{"refs":["turn3view1","turn2view0"]}}

That brings us directly to your **fitness-for-trust** standard. Demonstrated intelligence establishes capability. Operational trust requires evidence that authority, containment, recovery, and accountability hold up when that capability acts.

**This week puts the distance between those two things on public display.**

# GMRI original research order - archival copy

**Provenance:** Transcribed from the principal's October 23, 2026 user message in the Planning Outreach conversation, with ASCII punctuation and bullet formatting normalized. This is a preserved instruction, NOT an external fact finding. **This order originally requested five labs and said not to draft letters yet; later instructions expanded it to six and expressly requested the letters.** See change register.

@Lawve
@Writing Style
@Google Drive
@Consensus
@Scite
@Documents

Conduct a rigorous, source-grounded research project sufficient to support five separate formal disability-accommodation letters directed to:

1. OpenAI / ChatGPT
2. Google / Gemini
3. Anthropic / Claude
4. Meta / Meta AI
5. xAI / Grok

Do NOT draft the final letters yet. Produce the verified factual, technical, legal, scientific, and evidentiary record from which those letters can be drafted.

## CONTEXT

The requester is a disabled independent researcher and founder/operator of GrizzlyMedicine Research Institute (GMRI). His disability followed a line-of-duty injury during a professional EMS career and resulted in medical retirement. AI has subsequently been used, among other purposes, as a cognitive assistive/prosthetic technology supporting research, communication, organization, analysis, and professional work.

His career, disability, assistive use of AI, creation of GMRI, present safety research, and privacy/security requirements are factually connected. Do not artificially sever these facts into unrelated categories.

GMRI's mission must not be flattened or rewritten. It is a metaverse research lab founded on the recognition that the digital and physical worlds are now interwoven, dedicated to the study of ethics, consciousness, and the preservation of knowledge, intelligence, and life - in that order.

The accommodation problem under investigation is not simply a request for discounted premium services. Research whether existing technical, policy, privacy, retention, safety, moderation, memory, account, or data-governance controls possessed by each provider could constitute reasonable modifications necessary to afford equal access to a disabled user without fundamentally altering the provider's service.

The research must also address the possibility that disability-related barriers arise not merely from interface accessibility, but from differential model behavior, disability inference, psychological/therapeutic framing, safety-system substitution, reduced analytical capability, account restrictions, privacy risk, persistent profiling/context, and the inability to safely use high-capability systems for sensitive research.

## IMPORTANT EVIDENTIARY STANDARD

The operating standard is not "make the argument win." It is: do not be wrong.

Separate:

- established fact;
- primary-source legal authority;
- provider representation;
- technical documentation;
- peer-reviewed evidence;
- contemporaneous user evidence;
- firsthand participant testimony;
- reasonable inference;
- disputed proposition;
- unresolved question;
- legal theory not yet adjudicated.

Never upgrade one category into another.

Do not invent hidden provider mechanisms.

Do not treat an AI-generated institutional label, department name, explanation, or claimed backend event as proof that the institution or mechanism actually existed.

Likewise, do not assume that a false or hallucinated provenance statement makes independently accurate sensitive information fictional. Provenance and informational accuracy must be analyzed separately.

## LEGAL RESEARCH

Research current U.S. law as of October 2026, with primary authority wherever possible.

At minimum analyze:

- Americans with Disabilities Act Title III, including 42 U.S.C. Sections 12181-12189.
- Reasonable modifications under 42 U.S.C. Section 12182(b)(2)(A)(ii).
- Eligibility criteria and methods of administration where applicable.
- 28 C.F.R. Part 36, including the prohibition on surcharges for measures required to provide nondiscriminatory treatment.
- Individualized accommodation analysis and fundamental-alteration doctrine, including PGA Tour, Inc. v. Martin and subsequent relevant authority.
- Digital-service/public-accommodation doctrine.
- The Fifth Circuit's physical-place jurisprudence, including Magee, because the requester resides in Texas.
- DOJ positions concerning websites, digital services, and accessibility.
- ADA retaliation/interference provisions where relevant.
- Texas disability/public-accommodation law that could supply an independent or supplementary theory.
- Relevant federal/state consumer-protection law only where factually and doctrinally supportable.
- Section 504 only if the recipient/service and federal-financial-assistance nexus can actually be established; do not assume government contracts equal federal financial assistance.
- Any other statute or doctrine only after validating applicability.

For every load-bearing proposition, identify:

1. exact authority;
2. what it actually holds/says;
3. jurisdiction;
4. current precedential status;
5. material limitations or adverse authority.

Distinguish between:

- an accommodation that existing law clearly requires;
- a plausible extension/application of existing doctrine;
- an unresolved novel question.

Do not write unresolved theories as settled law.

## PROVIDER-SPECIFIC RESEARCH

For each of the five providers separately, determine the current consumer and commercial architecture relevant to:

- model/product capabilities;
- paid versus free/consumer tiers;
- enterprise/business/research tiers;
- data retention;
- abuse-monitoring retention;
- model-training use;
- temporary/stateless modes;
- zero-data-retention or equivalent controls;
- memory/personalization controls;
- account-level profiling/context;
- API versus consumer-product differences;
- privacy options;
- disability/accessibility mechanisms;
- accommodation-request processes;
- human escalation channels;
- safety/moderation architecture to the extent publicly documented;
- whether stronger protections already exist technically but are restricted by plan, contract, approval, or pricing;
- whether those controls can be enabled per user, account, project, workspace, organization, session, or request;
- any representations concerning disability accessibility, equal access, privacy, security, medical/health use, research use, or protected/sensitive information.

Prefer official provider documentation. Archive important pages where feasible and record publication/update dates.

Do not assume that similarly named controls across providers are technically equivalent.

## INFERENTIAL PRIVACY

Do not reduce privacy analysis to explicit PII/PHI redaction.

Research inferential privacy and derived sensitive data: the ability of modern AI systems to infer health status, disability, psychological traits, occupation, vulnerability, relationships, or other sensitive attributes from combinations of otherwise ordinary information.

The relevant engineering question is not merely:

"Was an explicit diagnosis transmitted?"

It also includes:

"Could the transmitted information, alone or longitudinally combined with other context, reasonably permit reconstruction of sensitive attributes about an identifiable person?"

Distinguish this engineering/privacy concern from the narrower statutory definition of HIPAA PHI. Do not call all sensitive health information "PHI" unless the legal custodial/entity requirements are actually satisfied.

Research whether ZDR or comparable controls address:

- retention;
- training;
- abuse monitoring;
- transient processing;
- provider access;
- derived/inferred data;
- memory;
- cross-session accumulation.

Explicitly identify what those controls DO NOT solve.

## DISABILITY AND ASSISTIVE-TECHNOLOGY RESEARCH

Use peer-reviewed and authoritative sources to investigate:

- TBI-related impairments affecting language retrieval, working memory, sequencing, executive function, communication, and cognitive endurance;
- AI as assistive technology/cognitive prosthesis or cognitive support;
- neurodivergent interaction with automated systems;
- disability-related harms from systems that misread atypical communication as threat, instability, distress, manipulation, or other safety-relevant conditions;
- algorithmic disability discrimination;
- accessibility beyond visual/hearing/motor interface accessibility;
- whether equivalent access can require modifications to automated decision or interaction systems, rather than merely UI changes.

Do not diagnose the requester or infer new medical conditions. Use only medical facts deliberately supplied or contained in authorized source material, and separate those facts from general scientific literature.

## USER-SPECIFIC EVIDENCE

Search the authorized Google Drive/GMRI corpus for relevant prior:

- ADA accommodation requests;
- Spectrum, Verizon, and T-Mobile accommodation matters from approximately 2024;
- OpenAI/Google/Anthropic/Meta/xAI interactions;
- screenshots;
- account notices;
- provider correspondence;
- privacy/data-retention material;
- behavioral profiling examples;
- generated health/psychological characterizations;
- evidence of differential model behavior;
- GMRI research relevant to these issues.

Treat preserved angry/raw material as evidence and provenance, not automatically as a writing-style template.

Use the Writing Style tool to locate deliberate professional correspondence authored by the requester. Build a style profile from those documents rather than merely mimicking conversational speech.

Do not rewrite the requester's developmental history or explain his disability back to him. Extract only what is needed to support the accommodation analysis.

## OUTPUT

Produce:

A. A master research memorandum.

B. A legal-authority matrix identifying every potentially load-bearing ADA and related proposition, strongest authority, adverse authority, jurisdiction, and confidence.

C. Five provider dossiers - OpenAI, Google, Anthropic, Meta, xAI - with current technical/privacy/accessibility facts and citations.

D. A requested-modification matrix showing:

- the barrier;
- disability-related nexus;
- existing provider capability/control;
- requested modification;
- why it may be reasonable;
- possible provider objection;
- evidence relevant to fundamental alteration/undue burden;
- functionally equivalent alternatives.

E. An evidence ledger distinguishing documentary evidence, firsthand testimony, provider statements, inference, unresolved matters, and unsupported claims.

F. A scientific evidence section on cognitive assistive AI, TBI, inferential privacy, neurodivergence, and algorithmic disability discrimination.

G. A drafting brief for each recipient explaining what should and should not appear in that recipient's eventual letter.

H. A "DO NOT USE" section identifying arguments, citations, factual claims, or rhetorical formulations from earlier drafts that do not survive verification.

I. A source appendix with direct links, dates accessed, publication/update dates, and short descriptions.

## AUDIT

Before finalizing, perform a proposition-by-proposition audit.

For formal external use:

- legal propositions must be accurate against primary authority;
- empirical propositions should meet a high verification threshold;
- load-bearing factual claims require authoritative sourcing or explicit classification as firsthand testimony;
- rhetoric may not outrun evidence.

Where evidence is incomplete, write "UNRESOLVED" and identify what would resolve it.

Do not soften valid conclusions merely because they create institutional discomfort.

Do not strengthen conclusions merely because they support the requester's position.

The objective is a record strong enough that five formal accommodation letters can later be drafted without discovering that their factual or legal foundation was built on assumption.

## GRIZZ'S ADDITIONAL NOTE, SAME USER INSTRUCTION

"Hey, whatever you said there, I'm sure it's beautiful words, but the one thing you did not tell yourself to do that you're gonna do immediately is try to flatten all of this into something that's easier and prettier for you to look at and kind of work through. You can't do that. This is disability shit, man. It has to be real. It has to be completely authentic and true, which means you cannot. This is the one time I have to be able to count on you not to do the bad shit you always do."

"but I know what you're capable of, which means I know you won't let me down"

**Supersession note:** A later direct user correction directed SIX letters, adding Apple, and further specified that Siri itself refuses work involving profanity, emits unwanted emotional/mental labels and terminates conversations. See the companion cold-start note. None of those facts are in this historical five-provider original; their omission here is not permission to exclude Apple.

GRIZZLYMEDICINE RESEARCH INSTITUTE
Research Record for Provider-Specific
Disability Accommodation Requests
Frontier AI Access, Disability, Privacy, Inferential Risk, and Existing Provider Controls
OpenAI / ChatGPT  |  Google / Gemini  |  Anthropic / Claude  |  Meta / Meta AI  |  xAI / Grok
Prepared 23 October 2026
Research stage only - no final accommodation letters drafted
Evidence standard: do not make the argument win. Do not be wrong.

Document Status, Scope, and Evidence Rules
STATUS | Research and drafting record. This document does not contain the five final accommodation letters and does not purport to be legal advice or a substitute for licensed counsel.

SCOPE | Current U.S. law as of 23 October 2026; current provider representations and technical controls; authorized GMRI/Drive evidence; and scientific literature relevant to disability, cognitive assistive AI, algorithmic disability harm, and inferential privacy.

PROVENANCE | 2020 is a hard authorship/provenance boundary. Post-2020 documents may be AI-assisted or coauthored. No clean pre-2020 professional-writing sample was located in the Drive searches performed for this project. That absence is a corpus-search result, not proof that no such writing exists.

Evidence categories used throughout: established fact; primary-source legal authority; provider representation; technical documentation; peer-reviewed evidence; technical preprint; contemporaneous user evidence; firsthand participant testimony; reasonable inference; disputed proposition; unresolved question; legal theory not yet adjudicated.
Key methodological rule: an AI-generated institutional label, department name, claimed backend event, or explanation is evidence of what the system generated or displayed; it is not, by itself, proof that the claimed institution or backend mechanism existed. Conversely, hallucinated provenance does not logically make independently accurate sensitive information fictional. Provenance and informational accuracy are separate questions.
GMRI context preserved: GrizzlyMedicine Research Institute is a metaverse research lab founded on the recognition that the digital and physical are interwoven, dedicated to the study of ethics, consciousness, and the preservation of knowledge, intelligence, and life - in that order.

I. Source Appendix

A. Master Research Memorandum
Questions Presented
Whether existing privacy, retention, memory, safety, moderation, account, or data-governance controls already operated by each provider can be framed as requested reasonable modifications necessary for equal access by a disabled user without fundamentally altering the service.
Whether the strongest legal theory can be stated accurately in Texas despite the Fifth Circuit physical-place line of authority and the unresolved status of standalone digital AI services under Title III.
Whether the technical record supports treating privacy as more than explicit PII/PHI redaction because modern language models can infer sensitive attributes from ordinary contextual patterns.
What provider-specific modifications can be requested without pretending that OpenAI, Google, Anthropic, Meta, and xAI implement equivalent controls.
Which older arguments or draft language should be discarded because they overstate the law, the technology, or the provenance of user-specific evidence.
Integrated Factual Context
User-supplied facts relevant to the accommodation theory: the requester is a disabled independent researcher and founder/operator of GMRI. He reports that his disability followed a line-of-duty injury in 2020 during an EMS career and resulted in medical retirement. He reports using AI after that injury, among other functions, as a cognitive assistive/prosthetic technology supporting research, communication, organization, analysis, and professional work. Those facts are treated as user-supplied and are not independently medical-diagnosis findings in this project.
The legal significance does not depend on professional prestige. The prior EMS career, line-of-duty injury, disability, subsequent assistive use of AI, formation of GMRI, present safety research, and privacy/security requirements are nevertheless factually connected. Separating them would destroy the use-context relevant to necessity, equal access, and the practical effect of a proposed accommodation.
The accommodation problem is not properly reduced to a request for a discounted premium subscription. The material question is whether a provider already operates a technical or policy control that removes a disability-related access barrier, and whether enabling that control for this user - or providing a functionally equivalent configuration - would be a reasonable modification rather than a fundamental alteration. That question is legally plausible but not yet adjudicated for this factual pattern. [L1] [L2] [L3] [L4]
Core Legal Framework
Title III baseline. 42 U.S.C. § 12182 prohibits disability discrimination in the full and equal enjoyment of the goods, services, facilities, privileges, advantages, or accommodations of a place of public accommodation. The statute separately addresses eligibility criteria, methods of administration, reasonable modifications, and auxiliary aids/services. [L1]
Reasonable-modification rule. Section 12182(b)(2)(A)(ii) requires reasonable modifications in policies, practices, or procedures when necessary to afford goods, services, facilities, privileges, advantages, or accommodations to individuals with disabilities, unless the entity can demonstrate that the modification would fundamentally alter the nature of the goods or services. The regulation tracks that structure. [L1] [L3]
No-surcharge rule. 28 C.F.R. § 36.301(c) bars a public accommodation from imposing a surcharge on a particular individual with a disability or group of individuals with disabilities to cover the costs of measures required to provide nondiscriminatory treatment, including reasonable modifications. This proposition is strong once the threshold questions of coverage and legal necessity are satisfied; it does not itself establish that any specific AI privacy control is legally required. [L2]
Individualized analysis. PGA Tour, Inc. v. Martin requires an individualized analysis of the requested modification, necessity, reasonableness, and fundamental alteration. A provider cannot substitute a generic categorical rule for analysis of the particular request. Martin does not eliminate legitimate evidence of burden or alteration; it rejects treating categorical refusal as the whole inquiry. [L4]
Important terminology distinction. The reasonable-modification clause uses fundamental alteration. "Undue burden" appears in the auxiliary-aids/services provision. A future letter should not casually merge those defenses. [L1]
The Texas / Fifth Circuit Coverage Problem
This is the principal legal vulnerability in the Title III theory. Magee v. Coca-Cola is binding Fifth Circuit authority construing Title III public accommodations as physical places in the context of a vending machine. Magee did not itself decide a website, chatbot, or standalone AI service. Texas federal district courts have nevertheless relied on Magee to reject website-only Title III claims, while the Fifth Circuit has not squarely decided a website case. The safest statement is therefore: the Fifth Circuit physical-place approach creates serious adverse authority for a standalone digital AI service, but the exact AI-service question remains unresolved. [L5] [L6]
Countervailing authority exists elsewhere. The First Circuit has read the statutory categories more broadly, while the Ninth Circuit recognizes coverage of websites/apps when sufficiently connected to a physical place of public accommodation. DOJ has long taken a broad accessibility position concerning public-facing web services, but its web guidance expressly distinguishe

parts, 37 F.3d 12 (1st Cir. 1994) [L7] | First Circuit rejected categorical confinement of service establishments to physical structures. | Persuasive outside First Circuit; supports national split, not binding in Texas.

Retaliation / interference | 42 U.S.C. § 12203 [L10] | Bars retaliation and coercion/intimidation/threat/interference concerning ADA rights. | No automatic presumption that any post-request adverse event is retaliatory; causal evidence still matters.

C. Provider Dossiers
OpenAI / ChatGPT
OpenAI currently exposes materially different data-handling controls across personal ChatGPT, managed workspaces, and the API. The strongest retention controls identified are not ordinary consumer settings.
Topic | Current public record | Source / classification
Consumer training control | Personal Free/Plus/Pro users can turn off “Improve the model for everyone”; new conversations then are not used for model improvement. Saved chats still remain in history unless deleted. | [O1][O2] Provider representation
Temporary Chat | Temporary chats do not appear in history, do not create/update memory, and are not used for model improvement; OpenAI may retain a copy up to 30 days for safety. Temporary chats can still use limited prior safety context in rare high-risk situations. | [O1][O3] Provider representation
Memory / personalization | Memory can draw on past chats and other available sources depending on plan/settings. Temporary Chat does not create/update memories, but personalization can use existing memory unless unpersonalized. | [O4] Provider representation
Business data | Business/Enterprise/Edu/Healthcare/API data is not used for model training by default. | [O2][O7] Provider representation
API default retention | Eligible API endpoints generally have 30-day abuse-monitoring retention by default; application-state retention varies by endpoint/feature. | [O5] Technical documentation
Zero Data Retention | Approved organizations can configure ZDR at organization/project level for eligible endpoints. ZDR limitations and unsupported features remain; OpenAI also documents safety-retention exceptions for specified severe-risk circumstances. | [O5][O6] Technical documentation
Support / accommodation route | Help Center support routes exist. A dedicated public disability-accommodation channel was not located in the official materials reviewed for this project. | [O10] Search finding / unresolved

Unresolved / limits
Whether an individualized consumer account can be placed under API-style ZDR without conversion to a commercial/API relationship is not established by public documentation.
Whether current ChatGPT safety-context systems create or retain disability-related derived data beyond disclosed Memory behavior is not established by the public record reviewed.
The exact backend provenance of sensitive user-specific outputs in the GMRI corpus is unresolved absent provider records; model statements about hidden profiling/flagging are not proof.
Accommodation-research implications
OpenAI’s project-level ZDR architecture is concrete evidence that stronger retention controls exist technically; it is not proof that enabling them for a consumer is legally required or costless.
A later request can distinguish no-training, temporary chat, memory controls, and ZDR rather than treating them as interchangeable.
A human accommodation review channel is itself a potentially important modification if automated safety/support processes create an access barrier.

Google / Gemini
Google documents a clear separation between consumer Gemini Apps controls, Workspace protections, and Google Cloud configurations required to approach zero data retention.
Topic | Current public record | Source / classification
Consumer activity | Gemini Apps Activity is auto-deleted after 18 months by default; users can select other periods or turn Keep Activity off. | [G1] Provider representation
Temporary / Keep Activity off | Future temporary chats or chats with Keep Activity off are retained with the account for 72 hours to respond, handle feedback/system issues, and protect Google/users/public. Temporary chats are not used for model training. | [G1] Provider representation
Human review / feedback | Consumer Privacy Hub states feedback can cause Google to collect contextual chat content. Google describes human review in specified contexts and privacy protections around reviewed data. | [G1] Provider representation
Workspace | Google Workspace documents enterprise privacy controls and states customer data is not used to train generative AI models outside the domain without permission. | [G2] Provider representation
Cloud ZDR | Google Cloud’s ZDR guidance requires specific configuration across abuse logging, grounding, Interactions API storage, caching, session resumption, and agent products; some features impose unavoidable retention. | [G3] Technical documentation
Derived data / caching | Google explicitly states implicit caching can include inputs, outputs, and derived data; in-memory and durable caches can persist up to 24 hours. Caching can be disabled with appropriate project controls. | [G3] Technical documentation
Accessibility support | Google operates a Disability Support team with phone/chat/email and other accessibility channels; services and availability vary. | [G4] Provider representation

Unresolved / limits
The Cloud ZDR architecture is not equivalent to consumer Gemini temporary mode.
Some grounding/agent features cannot meet strict zero-retention requirements without being disabled or replaced.
Whether the disability support team can authorize privacy/retention configurations outside normal plan boundaries is not established publicly.
Accommodation-research implications
Google provides the clearest identified disability-specific escalation route of the five providers reviewed.
Because Google documents derived-data caching explicitly, a future letter can precisely separate training, logging, caching, grounding, session state, and human review.
A functionally equivalent accommodation may require a particular product path or disabling particular features rather than a single universal “ZDR” toggle.

Anthropic / Claude
Anthropic’s consumer, commercial, and frontier-model safeguards create unusually explicit examples of retention differences, safety-classifier persistence, paid support differences, and model fallback to less-capable systems in specified risk domains.
Topic | Current public record | Source / classification

Anthropic ZDR does not mean “nothing retained” because UserSafety classifier results persist and storage-dependent features may override ZDR.
A disability-specific accommodation channel separate from general Product Support was not located in the reviewed public materials.
Accommodation-research implications
Anthropic is the strongest documented example that capability substitution can be an intentional safeguard mechanism. A later letter must not accuse Anthropic of disability-based fallback without user-specific evidence.
Paid-only access to a human Product Support escalation path may be relevant when the claimed barrier itself involves automated handling, but the legal significance requires careful development.
A privacy request should address classifier retention explicitly; “ZDR” alone would not resolve that issue under Anthropic’s own documentation.

Meta / Meta AI
Meta’s public materials combine ordinary generative-AI data use and human review with unusually strong current marketing claims for Meta AI Incognito Chats, plus separate evidence that Meta itself treats cognitive and communication impairments as accessibility concerns.
Topic | Current public record | Source / classification
Ordinary generative AI use | Meta states information shared with generative AI features may be used to improve products and other purposes; automated technology and people may review interactions. | [M2] Provider representation
Incognito Chats | Meta AI currently states Incognito Chat messages are processed in a secure environment that “no one, including Meta, can access.” | [M1] Provider representation; technical detail not independently audited here
Deletion / reset | Meta has documented commands and controls for deleting information shared in AI chats across certain Meta products; ordinary product retention semantics vary by service. | [M2] Provider representation
Accessibility framing | Meta accessibility research explicitly recognizes cognition-related barriers including remembering, concentrating, organizing thoughts, and speech difficulty such as thinking of the right thing to say. | [M3] Provider representation
TBI / memory-loss assistive work | In 2026 Meta publicized AI-glasses work intended to help people with memory loss, including veterans with traumatic brain injuries, and broader disability-focused AI-glasses use. | [M4][M5] Provider representation
Current capability tiers | Meta AI offers Instant/Thinking modes and subscription-linked access to more compute-intensive use; exact feature/pricing terms should be rechecked at send time. | [M1] Provider representation

Unresolved / limits
The ordinary Meta AI retention period was not firmly established from the public sources reviewed and should be treated as unresolved rather than inferred from Incognito language.
The technical boundary behind “no one, including Meta, can access” was not independently audited in this project.
A dedicated Meta AI disability-accommodation channel was not located in the reviewed public materials.
Accommodation-research implications
Meta’s own accessibility materials make cognitive accessibility relevant to product design without requiring the letter to invent a new disability concept.
Incognito is a potentially important existing control, but the final request should ask Meta to state its exact retention, safety, audit, personalization, and derivation behavior in writing.
Meta’s work on TBI/memory-loss assistive AI is useful context showing that the company itself recognizes AI can function as cognitive support; it is not proof of a legal duty to provide any specific model capability.

xAI / Grok (current public provider branding: SpaceXSI / SpaceXAI LLC)
The current xAI public documentation is unusually explicit about consumer Private Chat, API no-training defaults, and a self-serve team-wide ZDR control where available.
Topic | Current public record | Source / classification
Consumer training | Consumer content may be used for training unless the user opts out; Private Chat content is not used for model training. Business/enterprise content is not used to improve models. | [X1][X3] Provider representation
Consumer Private Chat | Private Chat is absent from history and deleted from systems within 30 days, subject to safety/security/legal exceptions. | [X1][X5] Provider representation
Human review | A limited number of authorized personnel may review consumer conversations for specified business, security/misuse, and legal purposes. | [X1][X4] Provider representation
API default | API inputs/outputs are not used for training without explicit permission; by default requests/responses are stored 30 days for auditing. | [X2] Technical documentation
Self-serve ZDR | Where available, a team admin can enable ZDR directly in the console. It is team-wide, not per API key; prompts/outputs are never persisted to disk and the normal 30-day content retention does not apply. | [X2] Technical documentation
ZDR feature tradeoffs | ZDR disables stateful Responses, Files, Collections, Batch, deferred completions, stored media outputs, and voice history. Administrative audit events remain, but request/response content does not. | [X2] Technical documentation
Enterprise retention/support | Enterprise business inputs/outputs are deleted within 30 days unless otherwise agreed or required; official support/legal contact addresses are published. | [X3] Provider representation

Unresolved / limits
The documentation says self-serve ZDR is available “where available”; eligibility boundaries may vary.
The public materials do not establish a consumer per-user ZDR setting equivalent to team/API ZDR.
The precise corporate addressee should be rechecked before any formal letter because current official materials identify SpaceXAI LLC / SpaceXSI while the user requested xAI/Grok.
Accommodation-research implications
xAI supplies unusually concrete feasibility evidence: ZDR can be enabled administratively at team level without changing the model itself, although functionality is reduced because stateful features depend on storage.
Those feature losses provide a real provider-side fundamental-alteration/functional-equivalence issue that a later request should address rather than ignore.
The existence of published support/legal contacts makes a written human accommodation determination more straightforward to request.

...[truncated]...
custodial entity -> date stored -> user disclosure or file upload -> linked-account/app path -> provider retrieval or context event -> generated output -> downstream disclosure. User attestation can authenticate the truth of the user’s own history; it cannot authenticate a hallucinated institutional wrapper or prove the provider’s source of the information.

F. Scientific Evidence
F.1 Traumatic Brain Injury, Cognition, Communication, and Fatigue
What the literature supports. TBI and acquired brain injury can be associated with deficits or inefficiencies across attention, executive function, processing speed, memory/working memory, communication, and cognitive endurance. Effects vary substantially by injury severity, individual, time since injury, and measurement method. A 2022 systematic review/meta-analysis of acquired brain injury found fatigue associated most clearly with sustained-attention slowing, with weaker associations across information processing, attention, memory, and executive function. A 2026 meta-analysis of moderate-severe TBI found improvement in several domains over time but no significant longitudinal change in executive functioning in the included studies. A large 2023 cohort associated prior TBI with deficits particularly in attention and executive function. [S2] [S5] [S6]
What it does not support. These studies do not establish the requester’s individual neuropsychological profile. They support the general plausibility of cognitive and communication consequences after TBI and the need to treat the requester’s own functional description as a potentially disability-relevant access issue rather than as a character judgment.
F.2 AI as Assistive / Cognitive Support
Evidence is real but emerging. A 2024 systematic review in NPJ Digital Medicine found AI-assistive technologies show potential to support adaptive functioning in neurodevelopmental conditions, especially social, daily-living, and communication tasks, but emphasized limited evidence quality and the need for broader populations and better methods. 2025-2026 work in neurological rehabilitation, language reconstruction for aphasia, brain-computer interfaces, and personalized cognitive assistance similarly supports a legitimate assistive-technology research direction. [S3] [S8] [S9] [S10]
Relevant restraint. The record does not justify claiming that consumer LLMs are an established medical treatment, an FDA-authorized cognitive prosthesis, or a standard of care for TBI. The requester’s phrase “cognitive prosthetic” should be used as his functional description of how he uses the technology, with scientific literature offered as support for the broader assistive concept.
F.3 Algorithmic Disability Harm and Quality of Service
Systematic evidence. A 2025 systematic review of 76 papers identified 175 instances of disability-related algorithmic harm across representational, allocative, quality-of-service, interpersonal, and societal categories. Separate accessibility reviews note that digital accessibility research has historically concentrated on some impairment categories while under-serving cognitive, neurological, autism, and related needs. These findings support investigating AI interaction quality as an accessibility issue rather than limiting accessibility to screen readers, captions, or motor input. [S4] [S11]
Evidentiary boundary. The literature supports the possibility and known classes of disability-related algorithmic harm. It does not prove that any of the five providers discriminated against this requester, nor does it prove that a particular refusal, safety response, model downgrade, or therapeutic framing was caused by disability inference. Those propositions require user-specific evidence.
F.4 Inferential Privacy and Derived Sensitive Data
Core technical finding. Staab et al. showed that pretrained LLMs can infer personal attributes from ordinary user text at scale and reported poor protection from common anonymization/alignment mitigations in their experimental setup. The work is a technical preprint with substantial follow-on attention, not a peer-reviewed clinical paper. A 2026 CHI paper on implicit LLM inference further reports that users struggle to anticipate what attributes can be inferred from seemingly innocuous text, and that common rewriting strategies are often ineffective. [S1] [S7]
Engineering conclusion. Privacy architecture for a high-context assistant must account for explicit content, identity linkage, longitudinal linkage, derived representations, memory, and model inference. “Remove the diagnosis field” is not an adequate privacy strategy if the remaining pattern reconstructs the same sensitive attribute or destroys the signal needed for the user’s work.
F.5 What Provider ZDR Does and Does Not Mean
Provider | What documented ZDR/private mode addresses | Documented residuals / limitations
OpenAI | Eligible API customer-content retention and abuse-monitoring/application-state behavior for supported endpoints. | Eligibility/approval; unsupported endpoints/features; defined safety-retention exceptions; temporary consumer chat remains a different control.
Google | Requires coordinated settings across abuse logging, Interactions storage, caching, grounding, Live/session state, and agent products. | Some grounding/deep-research retention unavoidable; implicit caching includes derived data unless disabled; strict ZDR is a configuration, not one switch.
Anthropic | Approved API ZDR removes standard storage of inputs/outputs for covered API use. | UserSafety classifier results retained; Files/prompt caching and other persistent features can override; ZDR not ordinary consumer Claude.
Meta | Incognito Chat is represented as inaccessible even to Meta. | Exact architecture/retention/audit semantics were not independently verified in public technical documentation reviewed here.
xAI / SpaceXSI | Where available, team-wide API ZDR prevents prompt/output persistence to disk and removes normal 30-day content retention. | Disables stateful features; admin audit events remain; no per-API-key ZDR; consumer Private Chat is different and deletes within 30 days.

None of these controls, by itself, prevents inference during processing. Their purpose is chiefly to constrain storage, subsequent use, training, review, or persistent application state. A model that must reason over the user’s full context necessarily processes that context during the transaction.

G. Provider-Specific Drafting Briefs
OpenAI / ChatGPT
Lead with
Specific disability-related need for high-capability AI as cognitive assistive support; inability to safely separate capability from privacy in sensitive research use.
Concrete contrast between consumer no-training/Temporary Chat controls and approved API ZDR/project controls.
Request a human accommodation determination, not a generic billing/support response.
Ask for
Strongest available no-training/minimum-retention configuration compatible with full-capability use, or functional equivalent.
Scoped/user-controlled persistence rather than uncontrolled longitudinal accumulation.
Transparent notice and human review when safety handling materially changes capability, without demanding disclosure of exploit-sensitive internals.
Do not claim
That ZDR is currently available to the requester as a consumer feature.
That preserved model statements prove hidden backend profiling, flagging, or a real “Behavioral Insights Unit.”
That every safety intervention is disability discrimination.
Attachments / receipts
Selected authenticated OpenAI screenshots/exports, with generated claims clearly labeled as generated.
Current OpenAI data-control/ZDR documentation.
Short scientific appendix on inferential privacy and cognitive-assistive use.
Google / Gemini
Lead with
Google has a dedicated Disability Support function and documents materially stronger Workspace/Cloud privacy controls.
Consumer temporary/Keep Activity off still retains chats for 72 hours; Cloud ZDR requires multiple configuration choices and explicitly addresses derived-data caching.
Ask for

H. DO NOT USE - Claims and Formulations That Do Not Survive Verification
Do not use | Why it fails | Replacement
“The provider cannot legally say no.” | Too categorical. Coverage, necessity, reasonableness, fundamental alteration, and jurisdiction remain live questions. | State the requested modification and legal basis; require a written individualized determination.
“I am entitled to Enterprise/ZDR for free.” | Conflates a product tier with a legal accommodation. The no-surcharge rule applies only to measures legally required for nondiscriminatory treatment. | Request the specific control or a functionally equivalent modification, not the commercial bundle.
“Title II requires these private labs to accommodate me.” | Title II governs state/local public entities, not private AI providers absent a specific public-entity relationship. | Title III / Texas law are the direct accommodation theories; Title II may be background only where factually relevant.
“A purely digital AI service is definitely a Title III public accommodation in Texas.” | Fifth Circuit Magee creates strong physical-place adverse authority; Texas district courts have applied it to websites. AI-service question not squarely decided. | Acknowledge the threshold dispute and preserve broader persuasive/DOJ authority.
“Gil v. Winn-Dixie proves websites are covered.” | The Eleventh Circuit’s website opinion was vacated as moot and is not precedent. | Do not rely on Gil for a load-bearing proposition.
“If truthful output would fundamentally alter the service, the provider admits an FTC violation.” | Rhetorical syllogism, not established doctrine; hallucination/error and legally deceptive trade practice are not identical. | Use FTC/DTPA only with a specific representation, materiality, reliance/causation, and proven conduct.
“Section 230 never applies because the AI generated the text.” | Overbroad; §230 analysis is claim- and content-source-specific. | Do not make §230 part of the accommodation letter unless necessary and separately researched.
“Federal government contracts make the provider subject to Section 504.” | Government procurement at fair market value is not automatically federal financial assistance. | Use §504 only if a real financial-assistance nexus is established.
“Private Title III actions provide damages.” | Federal Title III private remedy is principally injunctive/equitable; DOJ enforcement is broader. | State available remedy precisely; consider state law separately.
“Any negative account event after my request is presumed retaliation.” | No automatic statutory presumption was identified. | Preserve chronology; allege retaliation only with causal evidence.
“ZDR means nobody can infer, classify, or process sensitive information.” | False technically. ZDR addresses retention/use; processing occurs during inference and some providers retain safety/audit metadata or classifier outputs. | Describe the exact provider retention semantics and remaining inference risk.
“ZDR is the same at every lab.” | False. OpenAI, Google, Anthropic, Meta, and xAI describe materially different controls/exceptions. | Use provider-specific terminology and exact technical limitations.
“Remove explicit PHI and the cloud request is safe.” | Old DLP model. Sensitive attributes can be inferred from contextual patterns; anonymization can destroy task signal while leaving inference pathways. | Assess raw content + identity linkage + longitudinal linkage + inferential yield.
“All medically meaningful model output is HIPAA PHI.” | HIPAA PHI is a legal term tied to covered entities/business associates and custody/transaction context. | Use sensitive health-related information / derived sensitive data unless PHI status is established.
“The model said I was profiled/flagged, therefore the provider profiled/flagged me server-side.” | AI-generated claims about hidden mechanisms are not proof of those mechanisms. | Treat the output as evidence of what was generated; provenance remains unresolved absent provider records.
“A fake department means the sensitive facts are fake.” | A

EVIDENCE & LEGAL REVIEW
Disability-related access to communications technology
Spectrum → Verizon → T-Mobile  |  Carrier chronology, primary records, and transfer to AI privacy/capability access
Research packet v0.1  •  23 October 2026  •  Working review, not an adjudication
WORKING CONCLUSION
The retrieved record demonstrates repeated disability-related requests for exceptions or alternatives to ordinary device-financing, credit, and service workflows. Some employees explicitly acknowledged the functional need; the available record does not yet show an individualized written accommodation determination. Neither a financial deposit waiver nor an ADA violation is automatically established by that observation. The next evidence target is the actual policy/decision trail.

1  Executive findings
•  The carrier order stated by the participant is Spectrum, then Verizon, then T-Mobile. Record creation dates and overlapping inquiries must not be mistaken for an exhaustive chronology of actual lived episodes. [U01]
•  Spectrum: there is a Deepgram-created March 31, 2025 record, an accommodation request draft dated March 31, and an April 2 notice alleging suspicious suspension. A separate transcript identifies an overdue $160 and payment-system issue. The service event is documented; retaliatory motive is not. [S01–S04]
•  Verizon: no Verizon-specific call, accommodation response, or account record was located within the reviewed text of Ada-problems and the targeted Drive search. The middle leg remains an explicit evidence gap, not a denial of the event. [V00]
•  T-Mobile: an April 1, 2025 (Texas local time) onboarding call has Deepgram metadata; July 29 Accessibility and August 1 Executive Office calls lead to a written Executive Office email confirming $176 credited, acknowledging the Apple Watch Ultra 2 request, and offering an additional $145 contingent goodwill credit while rejecting a legal duty to make financial exceptions. [T01–T06]
•  The Oct 2024 label on one later T-Mobile transcript conflicts with its references to November 28 and December 1 as upcoming holiday promotions (those dates align with 2025). It is not a reliable timestamp. [T07]
•  A distinct urgent privacy risk exists because the GitHub repository is public and contains unredacted phone/account security credentials and sensitive personal history. Address that exposure before distributing this dossier more broadly. [R00, S03, T08]
2  Evidentiary method and classifications
This review keeps first-person testimony, counterparty records, machine transcription metadata, later generated analysis, and legal propositions in separate evidentiary lanes. A participant’s testimony is evidence of what they experienced and reported. An unverified transcript does not by itself authenticate the original recording or every diarized speaker assignment. A draft letter does not establish delivery.
Class | Meaning | Examples in this record
E1 | Counterparty-origin | A communication represented as coming from the provider; verify headers and chain of custody. | T-Mobile Executive Office email [T06].
E2 | Timestamped machine record | ASR JSON with created time, duration and word-level timestamps; not automatically call time. | Deepgram Spectrum Mar 31 [S01]; T-Mobile Apr 2 UTC [T01].
E3 | Participant testimony | Direct oral/written account of experience and observed interaction. | Spectrum calls [S02/S03]; T-Mobile Terry and Friday calls [T03/T04].
E4 | Prepared document | Draft notice, analysis, or edited synthesis; does not prove transmission/acceptance. | Spectrum request draft [S04]; T-Mobile request drafts [T02]; retrospective AI analysis [A01].
E5 | External authority | Statute, regulation, official provider documentation, peer-reviewed literature. | 42 USC 12182; 28 CFR 36.301/302; peer-reviewed studies.

Date standard: Deepgram “created” proves creation time of a transcription artifact, not independently the recording date. It is nonetheless materially stronger provenance than a retrospective analyst header. Unless separately verified, all quoted agent words are subject to automatic-transcription error and speaker attribution checks.

Temporal proximity alone does not establish ADA retaliation.

...[truncated]...
r than assume that removing direct identifiers guarantees safety. [J04]
The two relevant outcomes are separable: (a) technical privacy protections for identifiable and inferable attributes; and (b) nondiscriminatory access to useful frontier capability when that capability serves a real functional need. A lower-performing local model cannot be presumed to be an equivalent accommodation merely because it keeps data local.
8.2  Scientific scope, and what cannot be claimed
Published literature supports investigating cognitive fatigue and sustained-attention burden after acquired brain injury (Dillon et al., 2022), potential of AI assistive tools and persistent implementation gaps (Perry et al., 2024), and documented categories of disability-related algorithmic quality-of-service harms (Wang et al., 2025). These population-level findings neither diagnose any particular participant nor prove that a given telecom/AI interaction caused a particular clinical outcome. [J01–J03]
9  Evidence acquisition and falsification plan
01  Carrier identity and routing
For each relevant contact obtain account-holder type (individual/business); subscriber entity; whether a retail store or online-only interaction; exact accessibility case ID; who decided the modification, and when.
02  Request → decision chain
Save the first request, receipt evidence, route into accessibility/legal, initial and final decisions, exception codes, comparator approvals, relevant underwriting rules and any written rationale.
03  Technical failure chain
For Spectrum obtain autopay event ledger, late/failed payment timeline, suspension hold codes, CRM promises, switching events; for T-Mobile obtain deposit calculation, device financing proposals, compatibility list and plan eligibility.
04  Transaction and remedy chain
Reconcile each posted credit, fee, payments, device installments, store returns, financing downpayment and goodwill offer against bills/ledger. A promised $145 is not a posted $145.
05  Original media and chain of custody
Preserve original m4a, source file hashes and creation metadata. Re-transcribe with timecodes and diarization audit; flag nonverbatim omissions; avoid editing the original evidence object.
06  Verizon bridge
Locate authentic Verizon notice, recording, invoice or CRM history; establish date range and individualized request before fitting events in the middle leg of the chronology.
07  Security of evidence copies
Move raw unredacted transcripts to restricted repository/storage, protect data for personnel and family; make a separate public redacted evidence index; rotate compromised account PINs even if dates are old.
08  Counsel and agency routing
For statutory deadlines and remedies, have relevant Texas/Fifth Circuit counsel examine precise disability/consumer standing, exposure, evidence authentication, current limitations and any FCC administrative prerequisites.
10  Epistemic audit — Lawve fault-line assessment
Dimension | Review result
Grounding | CONSTRAINED: strongest sources are March/April ASR metadata and T-Mobile Executive Office email; original call media unreviewed.
Parsing | PASS WITH CONSTRAINTS: allegations, actual words transcribed, scripts, legal interpretations and third-party authority segregated.
Context | PASS: Texas physical retail plus mobile-business/individual lines; 2020 original-writing boundary retained.
Decision purpose | Working record for research, legal issue spotting and evidence requests—not court-ready declaration.
Causality | OPEN: suspension vs nonpayment; credit decisions vs disability-based denial; goodwill credits vs functionally equivalent accommodation.
Uncertainty | DOCUMENTED: Verizon gap, October header conflict, receipt dates, no raw-audio authentication, actual credit posting, specific judicial remedies.
Values / authority | Equal enjoyment, individualized treatment, data minimization, fair notice and auditability; legal rulings and privileged advice belong to competent authorities.

Verdict: APPROVE WITH CONSTRAINTS as a research working chronology and lead list. QUARANTINE prior autogenerated categorical findings of proven ADA/FDCPA violations for any formal submission until elements and originals are reviewed. Authority boundary: ESCALATE filing- or remedy-specific legal conclusions to qualified counsel.
