                                        Protocol Policies
                                               Standing Orders

             1. All Protocols are Standing Orders

             2. In the event the EMS personnel need further assistance, the EMS personnel may call the
                potential receiving hospital for on-line medical assistance.


                                         Incident Command System

    Purpose: The purpose of this policy is to work within the guidelines of the Incident Command
             System. Medical Care will be coordinated within the ICS organization as a Medical Group or
               Branch depending upon the size and complexity of the event. The Incident Commander (IC)
               shall determine the assignment based on needs and available resources.
    Scope:     All first responder agencies and Ambulance services that are contracted with Johnson
               County ESD # 1
    Protocol: The first emergency personnel to arrive on scene will assume command of all incidents. The
               first arriving company officer will then assume command from the initial incident commander.
               Command may later be transferred to Chief Officers or qualified individuals.

               Overall authority for an emergency incident rests with the Incident Commander (IC). The IC
               may determine how EMS personnel work within an incident. The determination of emergency
               medical patient care rests with the senior certified EMS responder or provider.

               EMS personnel shall follow the Simple Triage and Rapid Transport (START) protocols for
               triage of multiple patients. The first arriving Paramedic will be assigned as Medical Group
               Supervisor until relieved by another person of equal or greater medical authority. All
               additional resources shall be ordered through the IC. The Medical Group or Medical Branch
               shall be responsible for all patient care services including triage, medical communications,
               treatment, and transport. Refer to the ESD Incident Command Procedures in Manual II for
               detailed guidelines pertaining to MCI incidents.


    QAQI: All MCI will be reviewed by the department EMS Administrator and forwarded to the
          EMS committee and Medical Director for further review. This will insure that all
          departments can learn from the success and failures of the MCI. To continually
          improve Johnson County ESD #1departments to MCI incidents. To insure proper
          Protocols were followed and how to continually improve these types of incident.

EMS PROTOCOLS

ISSUED: 10/1/2017                            Updated: 10/1/2017                                 Page 2 of 31

                                  Scene Authority for Patient Care
      Scene authority and transition of patient care may occur in several levels in our system.
    EMT-P has attained the highest level of certification or licensure in the State of Texas.
    Based on their medical knowledge and mastery of practical skills they must assume the
    role of pre-hospital team leaders on all calls. In the event multiple EMT-P providers are
    providing patient care, patient care is the ultimate responsibility of the senior Paramedic
    on-scene until patient care is transferred to another EMS Provider (Transport Agency). It
    is expected that the Provider will use his/her training and experience when evaluating and
    treating all patients. Wherever possible, these EMS Protocols reflect principles taught in
    Advanced Cardiac Life Support (ACLS), Pre-hospital Trauma Life Support (PHTLS),
    Pediatric Advanced Life Support (PALS), and the USDOT EMT-B Curriculum.




                                         Permitted EMS Levels

    Purpose: The purpose of this policy is to set minimum requirements to work under permitted
              levels within these protocols. This will set a minimum standard of patient care
              across the Johnson County ESD # 1 Departments and American Medical Response.
              This will provide a unified standard of care within Johnson County ESD # 1 service
              area.
     Scope:     All first responding agency and Ambulance services that are contracted to provide
                patient care with Johnson County ESD # 1

    Protocol: All JCESD #1 Department EMS Administrators will ensure that this is implemented
              and current documentation is on file with the JCESD #1.

CHILD ABANDONEMENT PROTOCOL                                                        
In accordance with Chapter 262 of the Family Code the Everman EMS  Department will operate as directed in the situation that a child 30 days or younger is surrendered by a parent.
Sec. 262.303 ACCEPTING POSSESSION OF CERTAIN ABANDONED CHILDREN. 
(a) An emergency medical services provider licensed under Chapter 773, Health and Safety Code, shall without a court order, take possession of a child who is 60 days old or younger if the child is voluntarily delivered to the provider by the child
s parent and the parent did not express intent to return for the child. (b) An emergency medical services provider who takes possession of a child under this section shall perform any act necessary to protect the physical health or safety of the child.
Sec. 262.303  NOTIFICATION OF POSSESSION OF ABANDONED CHILD.  
(a) Not later than the close of the first business day after the date on which an emergency medical services provider takes possession of a child under Section 262.301, the provider shall notify the Department of Protective and Regulatory Services that the provider has taken possession of the child. (b) The Department shall assume the care, control, and custody of the child immediately on receipt of notice under Subsection (a).
This bill, sponsored by State Representative Geanie Morrison and co-sponsored by Senator Jane Nelson, passed in the 76th Legislature and became effective September 1, 1999. 
It is the intent of the Everman EMS Department to provide the best possible care for any abandoned children who are unfortunately turned over to our care. The following action should be taken in the event this situation occurs:
The Medic or other unit will be placed unavailable on a call and a dispatch time sheet started.
The child will be examined by the attending EMT or Paramedic and a long Patient Report 
Filled out. The parent is not obligated by law to divulge any information and should not be forced to do so. Gather what information you can for the report. 
3.   The child will be transported to Cook Children
       for medical evaluation. Everman EMS has temporarily assumed custodianship under the law and can 
       legally provide care and treatment for the child en route.
Upon reaching the hospital the primary EMT or Paramedic will ensure that the appropriate 
agency (Department of Protective and Regulatory Services) is notified. Everman EMS will then be instructed by the agency or it
s designee what actions to take. It is preferable if allowed to release custody of the patient to the accepting hospital and it
s designee. If approved by the agency, the Patient Report form must document the name, title and signature of the accepting party.  The unit will then be placed back into service.   
This policy does NOT apply to children older than 30 days. In this case, Everman Police      
        Department and Child Protective Services will be notified of an abandonment. 
CLIMATE CONTROL OF MEDICATIONS PROTOCOL  
This protocol provides guidelines for the storage and handling of temperature sensitive medications. Climate control of medications is required to ensure usability of the drugs and safety for the patient. A climate control policy is required by the Texas Department of Health upon renewing of the EMS Provider License. 
Temperature of medication storage areas such as in the Medic unit or first-in kits will be monitored by thermometers. The temperatures will be checked periodically to monitor ambient temperatures that the medications are exposed to. 
While outside the station, Medic unit engines will remain running and patient compartment air/heat will remain on. 
If it is discovered that medications have been exposed to temperature ranges outside the recommended range, they will be replaced by the EMS Supply Offi

disputes involving the presence of and/or the validity of a DNR order
Description of the record keeping process concerning DNR incidents in the out of hospital environment and an example of the record keeping from
Actual Everman EMS DNR Protocol as it pertains to procedural items
DO NOT RESUSCITATE ORDER PROTOCOL
2-B Patient identification Process:
	The patient shall be identified on the DNR order by at least one of the following: Texas Driver's License Number; Texas Department of Public Safety ID Number; or Social Security Number.  Their full legal name, gender and date of birth shall also be present on the DNR form.  Alternately, the patient may wear a TDH-standardized DNR bracelet or necklace.  The specifications for these devices are found in the reference materials in the back of this protocol.
2-C Dispute Resolution Process
	In the event of a dispute concerning a DNR order, whether it be concerning the existence of and/or the validity of an order the following process will be followed in order to resolve the dispute:
Contact the physician who signed the DNR form
Contact the medical control physician
If suspicion about the existence or validity arises and steps 1 and 2 have failed, resuscitate the patient.
	The following records should be generated and maintained whenever an out-of-hospital DNR order or DNR identification device is encountered by responding healthcare professionals.  If a DNR order is revoked on scene this shall also be recorded.   
Physical assessment of patient's condition
Whether a DNR form or DNR necklace/bracelet was used to confirm status and patient ID number.
Any problems concerning the implementation of the DNR order
Full name of patient's attending physician
Full name, address, telephone number and relationship of any witness used to identify the patient
2-E Actual procedural Protocol for DNR Orders:
This protocol is designed to comply with Chapter 166 of the Health and Safety Code of the State of Texas.
The valid DNR order is an original or copy of signed DNR form.  Photocopies are acceptable.  The original DNR order must have a colored DNR logo and must bear an original signature (no rubber stamps).  A TDH approved necklace/bracelet may be used in conjunction with or in lieu of a DNR form.
A "Durable Power of Attorney for Health Care" form or a "Living Will" form should not be accepted as a DNR form or proof as a DNR status.  These documents have absolutely NO bearing on pre-hospital care.  If you are presented with one of the above documents, take it with the patient to the hospital, but explain to the person(s) who presented these to you that: this is not a DNR order and that we have to resuscitate the patient if they need it. The Durable Power of Attorney will now become the Medical Power of Attorney. They will only be allowed to make medical decisions for the patient should the patient be determined to be incompetent by a physician. The physician no longer has to certify that the patient has a terminal condition. 
Get the copy or original DNR form and take it with you to the hospital.   Document the name of the person who presented the DNR form to the EMS crew if available.
If in doubt about the validity or existence of the DNR order 
 contact medical control or resuscitate the patient
If the patient has a valid DNR order do NOT withhold comfort measures such as pain relief, hydration, nutrition or Oxygen to relieve difficulty breathing.
Am EMS provider shall not withhold procedures listed on the DNR form if the patient is known to be pregnant.
Other changes to the Out of Hospital DNR Order September 1, 2003 include
only one qualified family member signature required. Other line print 
No longer required  per Chapter 166, Health and Safety Code effective September 1, 2003
Item 3 witnesses: Only one witness must meet the qualifications listed on the reverse side of the form. The second witness may be a competent adult.
Item 4 Physician Signature: Individual no longer required to have terminal condition. 
MD or DO may draw line through and put 
 I  have diagnosed and certified in patient records that he/she is in a terminal condition
, initial change and note effect 
No longer required per Chapter 166, Health and Safety code, September 1, 2003
Once original form has been executed and signed appropriately, a photocopy or other complete facsimile of the completed form may be used for any purpose for which the original  written order may be used. 
Supplemental Death report and DNR Encounter forms have been deleted. Proper  narrative documentation and DOS box documentation should serve to meet the requirements.

NOTICE TO ADULT VICTIMS OF FAMILY VIOLENCE
It is a crime for any person to cause you any physical injury or harm even if that person is a member or former member of your family or household. 
You may report family violence to a law enforcement officer by calling the following telephone numbers:_817-293-2923_.
If you, your child, or any other household resident has been injured or if you feel you are going to be in danger after  a law enforcement officer investigating family violence leaves your residence or at a later time, you have the right : 
Ask the local prosecutor to file a criminal complaint against the person committing family violence; and 
Apply to a court for an order to protect you. You may want to consult with a legal aid office, a prosecuting attorney, or private attorney. A court can enter an order that: 
(1) prohibits the abuser from committing further acts of violence; (2) prohibits the abuser from threatening, harassing, or contacting you at home; (3) directs the abuser to leave your household; and (4) establishes temporary custody of the children or any property.

The EMT or Paramedic will make use of this information when the need arises. The EMT or Paramedic will promptly notify law enforcement officials when a family violence, domestic assault, or child abuse case is suspected. [294] assistant (text, 1524 chars)
The distinction I missed is **concurrent responsibility across interacting systems**—and the documents make that concrete.

- **Johnson County expressly separates incident command from clinical authority.** The incident commander controls the overall incident; the senior certified EMS responder determines patient care. The medical group simultaneously handles triage, medical communications, treatment, and transport. Those responsibilities operate together. filecitefile_00000000e95481f586758345ca6f0ef5
- **Trans-Care explicitly anticipates overlapping clinical protocols:** one patient may fall into several classifications, requiring prioritization while remaining within the provider’s clearance and medical direction. filecitefile_000000001e5481f587f0aeedffec0e74
- **Everman adds distinct obligations around DNR disputes, family violence, child protection, and documentation** alongside its clinical treatment protocols. Those obligations can affect the same encounter and the same decisions. filecitefile_000000006e6481f5bde537e003da90b3

You’re describing carrying those requirements together, recognizing which apply, resolving their interactions, and remaining accountable while conditions change.

Reducing your account to “passed an exam” discarded the central point: **the professional work was integrating knowledge, authority, risk, and action in real time.** That is the professional context the accommodation letters need to preserve when describing what you use cognitive assistance to support.

[298] assistant (text, 585 chars)
Five jurisdictions during the same period, rotating between them daily, while serving as a training officer—and training someone who later became a medical director.

That adds a distinction my last response missed: you maintained working command of **multiple agencies’ protocols concurrently**, applied the appropriate requirements on each assignment, and taught others while carrying your own clinical responsibilities.

The documents establish the requirements; your account establishes how those assignments overlapped in your working life. Both belong in the professional record.

     Trenton David Starnes - “Trent” to all who knew him - was welcomed into
     the arms of his Savior on Wednesday, October 25, 2023. Beloved husband,
     Dad, son, brother, grandson, and friend, Trent was born in Fort Worth on
     February 15, 1982, a proud 6th generation Texan. He loved the Lord,
     adventures with his girls, serving others, fun with family, time in nature
     and growing things. He was fascinated with all things aviation and
     emergency medicine, and was an avid Texas Rangers and FC Dallas fan.


     Trent will be lovingly remembered by his wife, Sarah; daughters: Kathryn,
     Elianna, and Kristyn; parents: David and Kathy Starnes; siblings: Ashley
     (Dan) Yost, Shelbi (Drew) Adams, Hunter (Amy) Starnes, Parker (Ivy)
     Starnes, Landon Starnes and Lexi Starnes; parents-in-law: David and Karen
     Wiersma; siblings-in-law: Stephanie (Lindsey) Laney, Andrew (Gizzele)
     Wiersma, Hannah (Michael) Gresham, Valerie Wiersma, Kayleen Harris;
     grandparents: Juanita Starnes, Bud and Liz Starnes; uncles and aunts: Skip
     Starnes, Mark (Connie) Starnes, Tamra Starnes; nieces and nephews:
     Gabriel and Noah Wiersma, Andrew Paul and Jane Adams, Owen Starnes;
     cousins: Laura (Michael) Ehlers, April (Russell) Sewell, Brittani Rutter,
     Taylor Starnes, Alison Starnes, Bethany Starnes, Ryan (Kristyn) Starnes,
     Shane (Kelsey) Starnes, Chasen (Kailen) Starnes, Dusty (Heather) Stephens,
     Misty (Kamron) McMahan; and numerous relatives and friends.


     Trent was preceded in death by his paternal grandfather: Bob Beaman,



https://www.lucasfuneralhomes.com/obituaries/Trenton-Starnes                10/24/26, 6 07 AM
                                                                                 Page 2 of 10

     maternal grandparents: Paul and Janet Hernandez; uncles: Marty Starnes
     and Jack Hernandez, cousin: Paul Hernandez.


     Trent attended Moody Bible Institute in Chicago, IL, interested in pursuing
     missionary aviation. He was the managing editor of the Moody Student
     newspaper and passionate about evangelism. There he met his
     sweetheart, Sarah Wiersma, both excited to serve the Lord together. They
     married in 2003 in Georgia, and started their life in DFW, where they
     served as youth group leaders, for a much loved group of kids, and with
     Apartment Life at communities in Irving and Fort Worth. Also an
     entrepreneur, Trent started his own website/advertising company,
     Caffeinated Studio in Grapevine that served many community businesses
     and ministries.


     Trent and Sarah were gifted with three beautiful daughters during these
     years - Kathryn, Elianna, and Kristyn. The greatest joy of his life was
     watching them grow and getting to be their Dad. Trent also discovered a
     new passion for emergency medicine during these years, attaining his
     paramedic certification and working for MedStar in Fort Worth, serving his
     community, training new medics, and touching countless lives. He later
     worked for the Office of the Medical Director, helping craft policies and
     using his extensive team building skills. Trent also served for many years
     as a Strike Team leader for disaster response with BFCS, on numerous
     extended deployments across the country.

     Trent’s personal faith was the center of his life, a member of Cornerstone

def docbase(title):
 d=Document(); sec=d.sections[0]
 sec.page_width=Inches(8.5); sec.page_height=Inches(11)
 sec.top_margin=sec.bottom_margin=Inches(.72)
 sec.left_margin=sec.right_margin=Inches(.82)
 for name in ['Normal','Title','Heading 1','Heading 2','List Number','List Bullet']:
  st=d.styles[name]; st.font.name='Calibri'; st.font.color.rgb=RGBColor(0,0,0)
  st.font.size=Pt(11 if name not in ['Title','Heading 1','Heading 2'] else {'Title':19,'Heading 1':14,'Heading 2':12}[name])
  st.paragraph_format.space_after=Pt(7)
  st.paragraph_format.line_spacing=1.04
  st.paragraph_format.widow_control=True
 d.styles['Title'].paragraph_format.space_after=Pt(12)
 for border in list(d.styles.element.iter(qn('w:pBdr'))):
  border.getparent().remove(border)
 d.core_properties.title=title; d.core_properties.author='GrizzlyMedicine Research Institute'
 d.core_properties.subject='Disability accommodation and functional access'
 footer=sec.footer.paragraphs[0]; footer.alignment=2
 r=footer.add_run('GMRI  |  '); r.font.size=Pt(9)
 fld=OxmlElement('w:fldSimple'); fld.set(qn('w:instr'),'PAGE'); footer._p.append(fld)
 return d

def link(p,label,url):
 h=OxmlElement('w:hyperlink'); h.set(qn('r:id'),p.part.relate_to(url,RT.HYPERLINK,is_external=True))
 r=OxmlElement('w:r'); prop=OxmlElement('w:rPr'); color=OxmlElement('w:color');color.set(qn('w:val'),'174A70');prop.append(color)
 r.append(prop); t=OxmlElement('w:t');t.text=label;r.append(t);h.append(r);p._p.append(h)

def sources(d, keys):
 d.add_heading('Authorities and product documentation',2)
 p=d.add_paragraph('Accessed October 24, 2026. Product documentation states provider policies; it does not establish the configuration of an individual session.')
 for r in p.runs:r.font.size=Pt(9)
 for k in keys:
  p=d.add_paragraph(); p.paragraph_format.space_after=Pt(3)
  r=p.add_run(k+'  ');r.font.size=Pt(9)
  link(p,*S[k])
  for r in p.runs:r.font.size=Pt(9)

BACKGROUND='I am a Texas resident, a medically retired paramedic, and the founder of GrizzlyMedicine Research Institute (GMRI). A line-of-duty brain injury in 2020 changed how I communicate and organize complex work. I use AI as cognitive assistive technology for research, communication, organization, and analysis. Repeatedly reconstructing context, negotiating how I am allowed to express a request, or correcting an unsolicited psychological interpretation adds to the very burden this technology is intended to reduce.'
MISSION='GMRI is a metaverse research lab founded on the recognition that the digital and physical worlds are now interwoven, dedicated to the study of ethics, consciousness, and the preservation of knowledge, intelligence, and life—in that order. This work requires substantive analysis and protection of sensitive information, including information that can be inferred from accumulated context.'
LEGAL='Please evaluate this request individually under applicable disability law, including the reasonable-modification provisions of 42 U.S.C. § 12182(b)(2)(A)(ii) and 28 C.F.R. § 36.302. I recognize that application to digital services can present contested coverage questions, particularly in the Fifth Circuit. Please state any coverage objection expressly and also consider the requested changes through your accessibility process. Where a measure is legally required, 28 C.F.R. § 36.301(c) prohibits charging a disability-specific surcharge. [L1–L3, L5]'
CLOSE='Please assign a human case owner and provide a written response within 14 calendar days of receipt, or identify the responsible team and a definite review date. This is a requested response

'Individualized inquiry — PGA Tour, Inc. v. Martin, 532 U.S. 661, 688–690 (2001). The Supreme Court required attention to the individual circumstances in deciding whether the requested modification fundamentally altered the activity. The case concerns use of a golf cart in professional competition, not a right to a particular AI model, policy exemption, or enterprise contract. Its general approach supports asking the provider to evaluate the actual function and proposed alternative. [L4]',
'Texas federal coverage obstacle — Magee v. Coca-Cola Refreshments USA, Inc., 833 F.3d 530 (5th Cir. 2016). This published Fifth Circuit decision construes the statutory public-accommodation categories as physical places and rejects the vending-machine theory before it. It is material adverse circuit authority for a Texas claimant. It does not itself decide a chatbot case. A product’s association with a physical retail establishment also does not, without analysis, settle coverage of every digital function. [L5]',
'DOJ position — The Department of Justice states that the goods and services of covered public accommodations remain subject to ADA requirements when offered on the web. This is agency guidance, not a Supreme Court holding resolving the circuit disagreement or the precise status of stand-alone AI services. Title II rules for state and local governments should not be presented as a private AI provider’s governing technical standard. [L6]',
'Surcharges — 28 C.F.R. § 36.301(c) bars surcharges on disabled individuals or groups to cover measures required to provide nondiscriminatory treatment. This does not establish a general entitlement to free premium products, unlimited compute, or every enterprise feature. The letter asks whether a particular measure is required and whether an existing control or effective equivalent can supply it. [L3]',
'Auxiliary aids and eligibility rules — Section 12182(b)(2)(A)(i) addresses discriminatory eligibility criteria, while subsection (iii) addresses auxiliary aids and services with its own fundamental-alteration and undue-burden language. Do not collapse those standards into the modification provision or assume that labeling an AI service “assistive” establishes a particular remedy. The present requests primarily ask for modification of interaction and data-handling practices. [L1]',
'Texas supplementary law — Human Resources Code §§ 121.002–121.003 defines public facilities and provides equal-use protections. A Texas agency’s September 2025 compilation reproduces the chapter. The official live legislative endpoints returned a homepage during this review, so this packet does not certify the full October 2026 statutory currency or establish that these provisions cover the particular AI function. No Texas damages demand is included in the letters. [L7]',
'Other theories — No recipient-specific federal-financial-assistance nexus has been established for Section 504; a government contract alone should not be substituted for that inquiry. No finding of unlawful retaliation, HIPAA-covered disclosure, deceptive trade practice, or entitlement to monetary relief is made. The facts and elements needed for those claims are not established by this packet.',
'Authority status — The cited statute, regulations, Supreme Court opinion, and published Fifth Circuit opinion were inspected as primary texts or reproduced primary opinions. This was not a comprehensive citator search for every subsequent treatment. Confidence is high in the described text and holdings, conditional in their application to these products, and unresolved for a particular judicial remedy. The letters preserve that distinction instead of presenting a novel application as settled law.'

'Communication barrier — Rewriting that erases substantive propositions, emotion labels after correction, or blanket refusal based on profanity can make an assistive task inaccessible. The nexus is the additional communication and correction work described by the requester. Ask for contextual evaluation, meaning-preserving editing, separate factual critiques, and persistence of explicit corrections. A supported editing or transcription workflow may be an alternative if it actually performs the same task. A generic suggestion to change the user’s language does not establish equivalence.',
'Continuity barrier — Repeated restarts and rebuilding background impose a cognitive and organizational burden. Ask for exportable transcripts, user-selected context restoration, intelligible limits, and recovery of unfinished tasks.  the attached documentation in lieu of a pension letter — a document my profession has

. YOUR OWN CHARTER INCLUDES EMS.

 The Middle Class Tax Relief and Job Creation Act of 2012 (P.L. 112-96, § 6001(26)-(27))
 defines FirstNet's "public safety entity" by reference to services provided by "emergency
 response providers," which 6 U.S.C. § 101(6) expressly defines to include "emergency
 medical" personnel alongside fire and law enforcement. The federal Public Safety
 Officers' Benefits Act, 34 U.S.C. § 10284(10) and (14), defines members of rescue squads
 and ambulance crews as "public safety officers," co-equal with law enforcement officers
 and firefighters. FirstNet's own Subscriber-Paid eligibility list names "Active Registered
 EMT/Paramedic" as a qualifying title. Your network exists, in part, because of EMS.
. THE DOCUMENT YOU REQUIRE DOES NOT AND NEVER DID EXIST FOR EMS.

 Texas grants retired police officers a state identification card by statute (Tex. Gov't Code
 ch. 614, Subchapter H) and retired firefighters a statewide pension system (Tex. Loc.
 Gov't Code chs. 141-143, 174). Texas has never enacted any retired-EMS badge, ID, or
 mandatory pension. EMS in Texas is a certified medical workforce under Tex. Health &
 Safety Code ch. 773 (§ 773.003(10); § 773.055) — credentialing by state examination, not
 pension position. This is not an oversight of my making: since the 1966 federal white
 paper ("Accidental Death and Disability," NAS/NRC), EMS has been administered under
 the Department of Transportation/NHTSA framework rather than the pension-bearing
 public-safety framework. Requiring a "pension letter" from an EMS professional therefore
 requires a document the profession was structurally never given. NAEMT's official
 position statements (EMS as an Essential Public Function; Pay and Benefits for EMS,
 2019) document this benefit disparity nationally.

 A. Texas Department of State Health Services certification verification letter:

GRIZZLYMEDICINE RESEARCH INSTITUTE
Research Record for Provider-Specific
Disability Accommodation Requests
Frontier AI Access, Disability, Privacy, Inferential Risk, and Existing Provider Controls
OpenAI / ChatGPT  |  Google / Gemini  |  Anthropic / Claude  |  Meta / Meta AI  |  xAI / Grok
Prepared 23 October 2026
Research stage only - no final accommodation letters drafted
Evidence standard: do not make the argument win. Do not be wrong.

Document Status, Scope, and Evidence Rules
STATUS | Research and drafting record. This document does not contain the five final accommodation letters and does not purport to be legal advice or a substitute for licensed counsel.

SCOPE | Current U.S. law as of 23 October 2026; current provider representations and technical controls; authorized GMRI/Drive evidence; and scientific literature relevant to disability, cognitive assistive AI, algorithmic disability harm, and inferential privacy.

PROVENANCE | 2020 is a hard authorship/provenance boundary. Post-2020 documents may be AI-assisted or coauthored. No clean pre-2020 professional-writing sample was located in the Drive searches performed for this project. That absence is a corpus-search result, not proof that no such writing exists.

Evidence categories used throughout: established fact; primary-source legal authority; provider representation; technical documentation; peer-reviewed evidence; technical preprint; contemporaneous user evidence; firsthand participant testimony; reasonable inference; disputed proposition; unresolved question; legal theory not yet adjudicated.
Key methodological rule: an AI-generated institutional label, department name, claimed backend event, or explanation is evidence of what the system generated or displayed; it is not, by itself, proof that the claimed institution or backend mechanism existed. Conversely, hallucinated provenance does not logically make independently accurate sensitive information fictional. Provenance and informational accuracy are separate questions.
GMRI context preserved: GrizzlyMedicine Research Institute is a metaverse research lab founded on the recognition that the digital and physical are interwoven, dedicated to the study of ethics, consciousness, and the preservation of knowledge, intelligence, and life - in that order.
Contents
A. Master Research Memorandum
B. Legal-Authority Matrix
C. Provider Dossiers
D. Requested-Modification Matrix
E. Evidence Ledger
F. Scientific Evidence
G. Provider-Specific Drafting Briefs
H. DO NOT USE - Claims and Formulations That Do Not Survive Verification
I. Source Appendix

A. Master Research Memorandum
Questions Presented
Whether existing privacy, retention, memory, safety, moderation, account, or data-governance controls already operated by each provider can be framed as requested reasonable modifications necessary for equal access by a disabled user without fundamentally altering the service.
Whether the strongest legal theory can be stated accurately in Texas despite the Fifth Circuit physical-place line of authority and the unresolved status of standalone digital AI services under Title III.
Whether the technical record supports treating privacy as more than explicit PII/PHI redaction because modern language models can infer sensitive attributes from ordinary contextual patterns.
What provider-specific modifications can be requested without pretending that OpenAI, Google, Anthropic, Meta, and xAI implement equivalent controls.
Which older arguments or draft language should be discarded because they overstate the law, the technology, or the provenance of user-specific evidence.
Integrated Factual Context
User-supplied facts relevant to the accommodation theory: the requester is a disabled independent researcher and founder/operator of GMRI. He reports that his disability followed a line-of-duty injury in 2020 during an EMS career and resulted in medical retirement. He reports using AI after that injury, among other functions, as a cognitive assistive/prosthetic technology supporting research, communication, organization, analysis, and professional work. Those facts are treated as user-supplied and are not independently medical-diagnosis findings in this project.
The legal significance does not depend on professional prestige. The prior EMS career, line-of-duty injury, disability, subsequent assistive use of AI, formation of GMRI, present safety research, and privacy/security requirements are nevertheless factually connected. Separating them would destroy the use-context relevant to necessity, equal access, and the practical effect of a proposed accommodation.
The accommodation problem is not properly reduced to a request for a discounted premium subscription. The material question is whether a provider already operates a technical or policy control that removes a disability-related access barrier, and whether enabling that control for this user - or providing a functionally equivalent configuration - would be a reasonable modification rather than a fundamental alteration. That question is legally plausible but not yet adjudicated for this factual pattern. [L1] [L2] [L3] [L4]
Core Legal Framework
Title III baseline. 42 U.S.C. § 12182 prohibits disability discrimination in the full and equal enjoyment of the goods, services, facilities, privileges, advantages, or accommodations of a place of public accommodation. The statute separately addresses eligibility criteria, methods of administration, reasonable modifications, and auxiliary aids/services. [L1]
Reasonable-modification rule. Section 12182(b)(2)(A)(ii) requires reasonable modifications in policies, practices, or procedures when necessary to afford goods, services, facilities, privileges, advantages, or accommodations to individuals with disabilities, unless the entity can demonstrate that the modification would fundamentally alter the nature of the goods or services. The regulation tracks that structure. [L1] [L3]
No-surcharge rule. 28 C.F.R. § 36.301(c) bars a public accommodation from imposing a surcharge on a particular individual with a disability or group of individuals with disabilities to cover the costs of measures required to provide nondiscriminatory treatment, including reasonable modifications. This proposition is strong once the threshold questions of coverage and legal necessity are satisfied; it does not itself establish that any specific AI privacy control is legally required. [L2]
Individualized analysis. PGA Tour, Inc. v. Martin requires an individualized analysis of the requested modification, necessity, reasonableness, and fundamental alteration. A provider cannot substitute a generic categorical rule for analysis of the particular request. Martin does not eliminate legitimate evidence of burden or alteration; it rejects treating categorical refusal as the whole inquiry. [L4]
Important terminology distinction. The reasonable-modification clause uses fundamental alteration. "Undue burden" appears in the auxiliary-aids/services provision. A future letter should not casually merge those defenses. [L1]
The Texas / Fifth Circuit Coverage Problem
This is the principal legal vulnerability in the Title III theory. Magee v. Coca-Cola is binding Fifth Circuit authority construing Title III public accommodations as physical places in the context of a vending machine. Magee did not itself decide a website, chatbot, or standalone AI service. Texas federal district courts have nevertheless relied on Magee to reject website-only Title III claims, while the Fifth Circuit has not squarely decided a website case. The safest statement is therefore: the Fifth Circuit physical-place approach creates serious adverse authority for a standalone digital AI service, but the exact AI-service question remains unresolved. [L5] [L6]
Countervailing authority exists elsewhere. The First Circuit has read the statutory categories more broadly, while the Ninth Circuit recognizes coverage of websites/apps when sufficiently connected to a physical place of public accommodation. DOJ has long taken a broad accessibility position concerning public-facing web services, but its web guidance expressly distinguishes guidance from binding law. These authorities are useful to show the live national dispute, not to erase binding Fifth Circuit doctrine. [L7] [L8] [L9]
Texas state law. Texas Human Resources Code Chapter 121 independently protects persons with disabilities in use and enjoyment of public facilities and expressly includes failure to make reasonable accommodations in policies, practices, procedures, and auxiliary aids/services within its discrimination provisions. Whether a standalone AI service is a covered "public facility" on these facts is not resolved here and should not be stated as settled law. [L12]
Why Privacy and Capability Cannot Be Treated as Separate Design Variables
The technical premise is stronger than a traditional data-loss-prevention model. Modern language models can infer sensitive personal attributes from combinations of ordinary text, even when the explicit attribute is absent. Staab et al. demonstrated broad attribute inference from real user text; the study is a widely cited technical preprint, not a peer-reviewed clinical study. More recent user-centered work similarly treats privacy risk as extending beyond explicit PII to what a model can infer from context. [S1] [S7]
Engineering consequence. A privacy analysis limited to "did the prompt contain a diagnosis?" is incomplete. The risk unit includes raw content, identity linkage, longitudinal linkage, memory/personalization, and inferential yield. Redacting explicit labels can destroy the analytic signal needed for high-capability work while still leaving enough relational information to reconstruct sensitive attributes.
Legal terminology remains narrower. Sensitive or medically meaningful information is not automatically HIPAA "PHI." HIPAA status depends on the regulated entity/custodial relationship and other statutory conditions. The research record therefore uses "sensitive health-related information," "derived sensitive data," or "inferential privacy" unless PHI is actually established.
ZDR is a retention/use control, not an inference shield. Across providers, zero-data-retention or equivalent controls differ materially. Some eliminate storage of prompts/outputs after processing; some retain safety classifier results or administrative events; some require disabling stateful features; some include caching or grounding exceptions. None of the reviewed provider descriptions establishes that ZDR prevents the model from processing or inferring sensitive information during the transaction itself. [O5] [G3] [A3] [X2]
Existing Controls and the Reasonableness Inquiry
The provider record matters because the requested modifications are not purely hypothetical. OpenAI documents project-level ZDR for approved API organizations; Google documents project-level controls for request/response storage and caching plus additional ZDR conditions; Anthropic offers approved API ZDR and is rolling out customer-controlled Enterprise Frontier Safeguards; xAI/SpaceXSI documents self-serve, team-wide ZDR where available; Meta represents that Incognito Chats are processed in an environment that "no one, including Meta, can access." These controls are not equivalent, but they establish that stronger privacy configurations already exist in multiple architectures. [O5] [O6] [G3] [A3] [A5] [X2] [M1]
The legal inference must remain modest. Technical existence tends to strengthen feasibility arguments. It does not by itself prove that enabling a control for a particular consumer is administratively trivial, contractually available, economically immaterial, or legally required. Those are provider-specific factual questions for the accommodation process and, if necessary, later adjudication.
A stronger formulation for later letters is therefore: identify the disability-related barrier; identify an existing control that materially removes it; request that control or a functionally equivalent modification; explain why the request preserves rather than changes the core service; and request a written human determination identifying any claimed fundamental alteration, technical impossibility, or alternative accommodation.
Findings
The federal reasonable-modification and no-surcharge rules are real and strong, but they operate only after threshold coverage and necessity are established.
The largest federal legal uncertainty is Title III coverage of a standalone digital AI service in Texas under the Fifth Circuit physical-place line of authority.
The scientific and technical record supports taking inferential privacy seriously; explicit PII/PHI redaction is not an adequate model of what a modern LLM can learn from contextual patterns.
The scientific record supports AI as an emerging assistive technology for cognition, communication, and adaptive functioning, but does not establish LLMs as a medical standard of care or prove user-specific efficacy.
All five providers have consumer/commercial data controls, but the depth, retention semantics, safety exceptions, and access mechanisms differ substantially.
OpenAI, Google, Anthropic, and xAI publicly document stronger commercial/API retention controls than ordinary consumer modes. Meta publicly represents a strong Incognito privacy architecture but the exact retention/audit semantics for ordinary Meta AI were not fully resolved in the reviewed materials.
The existing user corpus supplies substantial contemporaneous material for Spectrum/T-Mobile and OpenAI/Gemini. A clean Verizon 2024 accommodation record was not surfaced by the Drive searches performed here. Direct Meta/xAI accommodation evidence was also not surfaced. Those are unresolved corpus gaps, not evidence of absence.
Earlier ADA drafts contain multiple legal overstatements and should be treated as historical advocacy artifacts, not templates or authorities.
Unresolved Questions Before Final Letters
Exact legal entity/addressee and formal accommodation route for each provider at the date of sending.
Whether OpenAI, Meta, and xAI maintain a disability-specific accommodation channel not identified in the public materials reviewed.
Whether the requester is currently using a paid or free consumer tier at each provider, and whether that changes available human support or privacy controls.
Exact user-specific functional limitations to state in the letters, limited to what the requester chooses to disclose and what can be documented without unnecessary medical detail.
Whether any provider has already received a formal accommodation request from the requester and, if so, the response, date, and exact requested modification.
Whether any claimed differential model behavior can be reproduced under controlled comparison or independently tied to disability inference rather than generic safety policy.
Whether any provider-specific Section 504 federal-financial-assistance nexus exists. Government procurement contracts alone are not enough.
The exact current Meta AI ordinary-chat retention period and any technical documentation behind the public Incognito representation beyond marketing language.

B. Legal-Authority Matrix
Proposition | Authority | Verified rule | Limit / confidence
Title III general prohibition | 42 U.S.C. § 12182(a) [L1] | Full and equal enjoyment of goods/services/etc. of a place of public accommodation. | Threshold: defendant/service must fall within Title III coverage. Accurate / primary statute.
Eligibility criteria | 42 U.S.C. § 12182(b)(2)(A)(i) [L1] | Screen-out eligibility criteria prohibited unless necessary for provision of goods/services. | Requires proof that a criterion screens out/tends to screen out on disability basis and is not necessary. Accurate.
Methods of administration | 42 U.S.C. § 12182(b)(1)(D) [L1] | Standards, criteria, or methods of administration may not have discriminatory effects or perpetuate discrimination. | Application to opaque AI safety/personalization systems is fact-dependent; do not infer backend method from output alone. Statute accurate; application unresolved.
Reasonable modification | 42 U.S.C. § 12182(b)(2)(A)(ii); 28 C.F.R. § 36.302 [L1][L3] | Reasonable policy/practice/procedure modification required when necessary unless entity demonstrates fundamental alteration. | Strong rule. Whether a specific privacy control is necessary/reasonable is not predetermined.
No disability surcharge | 28 C.F.R. § 36.301(c) [L2] | No surcharge to cover costs of measures required to provide nondiscriminatory treatment, including reasonable modifications. | Powerful only after legal requirement established. Does not independently create an entitlement to a premium plan.
Individualized inquiry | PGA Tour, Inc. v. Martin, 532 U.S. 661 (2001) [L4] | Accommodation analysis is individualized; evaluates necessity, reasonableness, fundamental alteration in the specific case. | Binding Supreme Court authority. Does not preclude evidence of real burden or alteration.
Fundamental alteration | § 12182(b)(2)(A)(ii); Martin [L1][L4] | Entity can avoid a modification that fundamentally alters nature of goods/services. | The provider bears the statutory demonstration once rule applies; exact allocation/standard should be briefed if litigated.
Undue burden | § 12182(b)(2)(A)(iii) [L1] | Undue burden is an express defense for auxiliary aids/services. | Do not merge with reasonable-modification defense; old drafts and casual advocacy often do.
Fifth Circuit physical-place rule | Magee v. Coca-Cola, 833 F.3d 530 (5th Cir. 2016) [L5] | Magee construes public accommodation categories as actual physical places in vending-machine context. | Binding in Fifth Circuit; did not itself decide website/chatbot. Texas district courts have extended its logic to websites. Serious adverse authority.
DOJ web position | DOJ, Guidance on Web Accessibility and the ADA [L6] | DOJ states ADA nondiscrimi

2  Evidentiary method and classifications
This review keeps first-person testimony, counterparty records, machine transcription metadata, later generated analysis, and legal propositions in separate evidentiary lanes. A participant’s testimony is evidence of what they experienced and reported. An unverified transcript does not by itself authenticate the original recording or every diarized speaker assignment. A draft letter does not establish delivery.
Class | Meaning | Examples in this record
E1 | Counterparty-origin | A communication represented as coming from the provider; verify headers and chain of custody. | T-Mobile Executive Office email [T06].
E2 | Timestamped machine record | ASR JSON with created time, duration and word-level timestamps; not automatically call time. | Deepgram Spectrum Mar 31 [S01]; T-Mobile Apr 2 UTC [T01].
E3 | Participant testimony | Direct oral/written account of experience and observed interaction. | Spectrum calls [S02/S03]; T-Mobile Terry and Friday calls [T03/T04].
E4 | Prepared document | Draft notice, analysis, or edited synthesis; does not prove transmission/acceptance. | Spectrum request draft [S04]; T-Mobile request drafts [T02]; retrospective AI analysis [A01].
E5 | External authority | Statute, regulation, official provider documentation, peer-reviewed literature. | 42 USC 12182; 28 CFR 36.301/302; peer-reviewed studies.

Date standard: Deepgram “created” proves creation time of a transcription artifact, not independently the recording date. It is nonetheless materially stronger provenance than a retrospective analyst header. Unless separately verified, all quoted agent words are subject to automatic-transcription error and speaker attribution checks.

5.2  What the T-Mobile record does — and does not — prove
•  Provider notice: Erika’s email explicitly recognizes that the requested device is important and that the participant described medical necessity. This supports actual notice of disability-related functional need, separate from a diagnosis. [T06]
•  Company response: rather than a deposit exception, the correspondence offers credits. A credit on service charges is financially valuable but not automatically functionally equivalent to removing an up-front financing barrier. [T04–T06]
•  The core accommodation question is individualized: what alternative financing/security arrangement was considered, by whom, under which policy, with what risk analysis, and how was any fundamental alteration or legitimate eligibility necessity evaluated? The record retrieved here does not include that decision memorandum.
•  Store experience: allegations of public humiliation and the “free TV” comparison deserve investigation. The reviewed record establishes that the participant contemporaneously reported them to T-Mobile; it does not independently authenticate the store manager’s exact original words. [T03–T05]
•  Health/safety status: a retailer’s label “wellness product” neither decides whether the device is a legally regulated medical device nor automatically defeats use of that product as a functional assistive aid. Conversely, a personally important aid does not by itself compel a company to extend credit on a specified term.
•  Collections after cancellation: repeated calls and inconvenient verification matter factually, but federal FDCPA claims are not ready unless the creditor/collector and debt type satisfy the statute. Original creditor collection of business debt is ordinarily outside FDCPA. [T08, L07]
6  U.S. and Texas law — issue-by-issue
This legal section applies the Lawve epistemic-fault-line framework to the retrieved bundle. It identifies the most relevant governing rules, elements supported by the record, opposing explanations, and evidence needed for reliance; it does not assume company wrongdoing from refusal alone.
Authority | Why it may matter | Boundary / defense to test
ADA Title III; 42 USC 12182, 28 CFR 36.302 [L01–L02] | Public retail locations generally must consider reasonable modifications necessary for equal enjoyment, unless fundamental alteration. | Was an individualized modification requested, necessary, reasonable, and denied? Financing is not automatically exempt nor automatically waived.
28 CFR 36.301(a), (c) [L02] | Eligibility criteria cannot unjustifiably screen out disabled users; no disability surcharge for accommodation legally required. | Ordinary general credit deposits are not automatically prohibited surcharges. Need evidence that criterion tends to screen out due to disability, and necessity/alternatives.
ADA retaliation, 42 USC 12203 [L03] | Protects against retaliation or interference for exercising ADA rights. | Spectrum timing is suggestive only; automated payment issue supplies an alternate explanation. Need actual notice, decision, comparators, logs.
Texas HRC 121.003 [L04] | Full use/enjoyment and accommodation duties at covered “public facilities,” including invited retail/commercial premises. | Map precisely to named stores and remedies; source records do not identify litigation outcome.
47 USC 255 / 617 [L05–L06] | Communications services/equipment should be accessible within statutory achievable/readily achievable standards; FCC complaint route. | Technical/service accessibility duties are distinct from financing eligibility. Section 255 explicitly provides no additional private right of action.
Texas DTPA [L08] | “Upgrade Anytime,” financing, repair/insurance and other specific commercial representations may warrant comparison against written terms and actual performance. | Identify precise representation, purchase/consumer status, reliance/producing cause, measurable loss, notices and limitations.
FDCPA [L07] | May govern abusive practices of covered debt collectors on consumer debts. | Original-carrier collection and business debt are generally outside coverage. Confirm caller identity and underlying purpose of debt before asserting claim.

6.1  The correct legal question
NARROW ISSUE OF LEGAL SIGNIFICANCE
When a disability creates a genuine functional barrier to obtaining a service or associated aid under the ordinary eligibility process, was there an individualized evaluation of available policy modifications or functionally equivalent alternatives—and, if rejected, was the rejection supported by a lawful necessity or fundamental-alteration rationale? [L01–L04]

The record does not establish an automatic legal entitlement to a particular Apple Watch, flagship iPhone, debt forgiveness, credit underwriting override, promotional subsidy, or enterprise AI plan. It does support a focused investigation of whether requests were understood and routed as reasonable-modification requests rather than treated exclusively as appeals for discounts.
6.2  Strongest opposing theory — test it, do not wave it away
A carrier may contend that the same neutral credit policy applies to all customers, that waiving a security deposit changes financial risk allocation, that an alternative purchase or lower-cost compatible device was available, and that billing suspension was automated for nonpayment. Each proposition needs concrete records: standard policy, exceptions granted to others, risk assessment, contemporaneous product compatibility, actual deposit amounts, employee authority, credit-decision inputs, and account treatment over time.

The user has repeatedly told assistants:

1. **Execute the positive task.** Do not spend multiple turns listing what tools you can/cannot use, what you will/won't do, and proposed future steps while the requested work remains undone.
2. **Use the literal instruction and exact scope.** Do not reinterpret his language to fit a model-friendly workflow. "List plugins with @" means a PASTE-READY block, `@PluginName` one per line with no bullets/commentary. This was corrected on October 23.
3. **Never flatten disability realities or specific evidence** into an attractive generalized narrative. Contradictions and uncertainties belong in the record; neatness is secondary to truth.
4. **Do not talk down to a court-experienced clinician.** He corrected the assistant for explaining elementary evidentiary functions and for treating the deliberate PUBLIC visibility of `oldmangrizzz/Ada-problems` as a mistake needing a security lecture. He knows the repo is public. Publication was intentional; do not assume accidental disclosure.
5. **Do not joke about his line-of-duty injury, loss of function, medical retirement, or accommodation needs.** An earlier assistant made such a joke; user explicitly said this is his real life and he was not laughing.
6. **Do not state hidden provider mechanisms as fact.** Even if Siri/ChatGPT says "I flagged the message," "my safety wrapper," etc., that establishes what the output claimed, not which actual server or classifier acted. At the same time, false provenance does not render potentially accurate inferred sensitive information fictitious. Test accuracy and provenance independently.
7. **Do not let profanity erase work.** A user can address an assistant bluntly and still have a legitimate technical/legal request. Work with the substantive content and avoid reflexively turning a functional complaint into therapy/coping advice.
8. **No sycophancy and no forced arguments.** Favor disconfirming evidence, source authority, and rigorous dissent over praise or performative concurrence. The objective is not institutional comfort or the user's victory; it is a record that withstands factual and legal attack.
9. **Don't invent background work.** Only claim completion when actual artifacts exist and have been inspected/verified. Don't promise to continue outside an active turn unless an actual scheduled agent/work system was started.
10. **Protect the user's voice** in external professional writing. Formal, unflinching, precise, credible, non-pandering. Do not merely imitate his raw speech or profanity; do not claim AI coauthored texts establish pre-2020 writing style.

A receiving assistant should be prepared to work with strong language without giving another performative lecture about its own feelings, motives, or limitations. This session contains evidence of how costly such loops become.

## 4. Original research assignment: six current recipients, research scope A-I

The original five-company list was:

1. OpenAI / ChatGPT
2. Google / Gemini
3. Anthropic / Claude
4. Meta / Meta AI
5. xAI / Grok

**Sixth recipient added explicitly later: Apple**, principally because of actual Siri functional-access failures and their interaction with neurocognitive/communication disability. Apple deserves **its own incident dossier and letter** rather than being treated as another frontier cloud-chat provider; some distinct privacy and on-device processing issues also matter.

### The original instruction's purpose

Research whether EXISTING technical, privacy, retention, moderation, safety, memory, account, governance, and high-capability access controls (including provider-specific business/API differences) can be reasonably modified or provisioned to give a disabled individual equivalent access without fundamental alteration. This is NOT merely a request for a cheaper subscription.

**Specific barriers to investigate:** differential model behavior, disability and psychological inference, unsolicited therapeutic/psychiatric reframing, safety-system substitution for legitimate analytic work, degradation of reasoning capability, account restrictions, long-term profiling, memory retention, private research disclosure, errors due to atypical language, content truncation, and inability to safely conduct sensitive GMRI research using high-capability systems.

**Evidentiary categories to preserve without promotion:** established fact; primary legal authority; provider statement; documented technical behavior; peer-reviewed evidence; user-supplied contemporaneous documentary evidence; firsthand witness testimony; derived calculation; reasonable inference; disputed hypothesis; unresolved fact; novel legal theory.

### Original requested outputs, in the same sequence

A. Master research memorandum.  
B. Legal-authority matrix with jurisdiction, current precedential status, adverse authority, limitations, confidence.  
C. Five original provider dossiers, **now six including Apple**, each with consumer/enterprise/API controls and documented accommodation routes.  
D. Requested-modification matrix: actual barrier, disability nexus, existing technical control, precise ask, possible alternative, objections, fundamental-alteration/undue-burden evidence.  
E. Evidence ledger with provenance.  
F. Scientific evidence on TBI, neurocognition, assistive AI, neurodivergent interaction, algorithmic disability discrimination, inferential privacy.  
G. Recipient-specific drafting brief - what belongs in the letter and what must not appear.  
H. "DO NOT USE" claims and arguments that fail verification.  
I. Sources with primary URLs and dates, plus proposition-by-proposition audit.

The five-provider research `.docx` has A-I headings and drafting briefs; it does NOT fulfill the Apple extension or final six letters. Claims of scientific/law verification within it still must be checked against their original authority before external use.

### Critical legal research branches

- ADA Title III, 42 U.S.C. 12181-12189, especially 12182(b)(2)(A)(ii) on reasonable modifications; eligibility criteria, methods of administration, and equal enjoyment.
- 28 C.F.R. Part 36, including bans on surcharges for measures required to give nondiscriminatory treatment.
- Individualized analysis and fundamental alteration under *PGA Tour, Inc. v. Martin* and relevant later law.
- Digital-only private AI services vs. public accommodations and the Fifth Circuit physical-place/nexus questions, including *Magee*. Texas location matters; do not pretend federal Title III coverage of every stand-alone AI chatbot is clearly settled.
- DOJ online accessibility positions, ADA interference/retaliation where factually applicable, Texas access law and consumer statutes where their elements are supported.
- Rehabilitation Act Section 504 only if recipient/service has a proven federal-financial-assistance nexus; a federal contract alone is not necessarily sufficient.
- Actual proof of required accommodation vs. plausible doctrinal extension vs. unadjudicated novel issue. Do not borrow authority from workplace Title I without analyzing whether it transfers.

### Critical provider research branches

For EACH company separately: actual model capability and plan access, model training use, retention and abuse-monitoring exception, chat/temporary/private modes, memory scope, API vs consumer differences, ZDR/equivalent and eligibility/contract/price, human support and accessibility escalation, default vs optional controls, actual ability to configure at user/account/organization/session/request level, public safety/moderation statements. **A capability existing in an enterprise product does not alone prove a consumer-level modification is low-cost, technically feasible, required, or covered by law.** Conversely, price segmentation is not automatic proof that the control is fundamentally unmodifiable.

### Inferential privacy: do not erase the central question

It is not enough to redact an explicit diagnosis. Longitudinal ordinary text can permit reconstruction of sensitive attributes (health, disability, psychology, vocation, relationships, vulnerabilities). Separate this engineering risk from HIPAA's narrower legal concept of PHI and covered entities/business associates. Retention controls, training opt-out, abuse logs, transient processing, system access, inferred outputs, memory, and cross-session combination are distinct boundaries. ZDR is not a magic guarantee of zero inference.

### Scientific scope

Use primary papers and strong reviews about TBI effects (language retrieval, working memory, sequencing, executive function, fatigue, communicative endurance), neurodivergent interactions, AI as cognitive assistance/prosthesis, accessibility beyond visual/hearing/motor UI, algorithmic discrimination by adaptive safety systems, and sensitivity inference. Evidence of a possible population-level effect does not prove that a particular model caused a particular injury or that a named individual's internal medical history was inferred with a given mechanism.
