## J Legal and evidentiary audit: material limits and next proof

The strongest present formulation is a request to remove demonstrated functional barriers through existing settings, supported workflows, or individually reviewed policy exceptions. Whether these measures are legally required for a particular provider remains conditional on coverage, disability-related necessity, reasonableness, and any proven defense. The letters do not demand immunity from substantive safety restrictions, a new model, guaranteed correct outputs, or unrestricted compute.

Equal treatment can require a change in practices, not only interface presentation: § 12182(b)(2)(A)(ii) and § 36.302(a) are not confined to screen readers or physical controls. Applying that rule to automated response behavior is a proposed application, not a reported AI-specific holding. Eligibility criteria are separately addressed by § 12182(b)(2)(A)(i) and § 36.301(a); discriminatory methods of administration by § 12182(b)(1)(D) and § 36.204. A neutral-looking classifier or approval rule is not established as discriminatory merely because it produces an unwelcome result. [L1–L3]

If an actual direct-threat justification is asserted, § 36.208 requires an individualized assessment grounded in current medical knowledge or the best available objective evidence, considering the risk and whether modifications mitigate it. This defense concerns significant risks to the health or safety of others. Ordinary policy restrictions, an automated score, and the statutory direct-threat defense are not interchangeable. No provider has made a verified direct-threat determination in this record. [L2]

Retaliation/interference — 42 U.S.C. § 12203 prohibits disability-rights retaliation and coercion, intimidation, threats, or interference within its terms. An account restriction after a request is chronology, not by itself causation. Preserve the request, proof of receipt, restriction notice, provider explanation, and timing before asserting a violation. No such claim is established here. [L12]

Remedy — Private Title III enforcement under § 12188(a) incorporates preventive relief; this is not a basis for demanding compensatory damages under Title III. A contemplated suit needs a defendant-specific analysis of standing, future use/injury, coverage, venue, applicable terms and any arbitration clause. These are unresolved litigation questions, not invented prerequisites to submitting the present accommodation requests. No letter has been sent in this work. [L11]

Section 504 and consumer theories — No provider-specific federal financial assistance or covered-program nexus has been established. Contracts alone do not supply that missing showing. Nor has this record established the elements, standing, causation, and remedy for a particular Texas consumer claim. Do not append generic statutory threats to fill that gap. These theories are reserved for separately supported analysis, not treated as adjudicated or disproved.

Authentication — FRE 901(a) asks whether there is sufficient evidence that an item is what its proponent claims. Participant knowledge and distinctive characteristics can help. Rules 902(13)–(14) provide specified certification routes for electronic records and copied data; a SHA-256 digest by itself is not the required certification. A digest documents byte identity relative to a preserved copy, not who generated an original response or whether its claims were true. Authentication alone does not resolve hearsay, relevance, completeness, or other admissibility questions. [E13–E14]

Preservation — Keep original bytes, capture dates and time zones when known, account/product surface, surrounding turns, native exports, and the method of capture. Preserve both successful and unsuccessful interactions. The evidence inventory identifies reviewed files, not an authenticated chain of custody predating receipt. No original has been altered or submitted externally. The packet excludes unnecessary third-party identities and underlying private source media.

Causation — The documented Apple exchanges establish visible interaction behavior. They do not independently identify the responsible backend model, classifier input, account-level profile, disability inference, or cause of a limit/error. General inferential-privacy research supports a risk analysis; it does not supply missing incident causation. Where a response reveals accurate sensitive information with false provenance, test the information and provenance separately.

Scope — This audit reconciles the six current letters, supporting dossier, cited legal texts, provider pages, selected professional records, and identified Apple media. It does not certify that every file in the larger GMRI corpus has been read or every proposition in prior drafts verified. No comprehensive commercial citator report or completed medical evaluation is represented. Unresolved entries remain visible in Proposition_Audit.csv.

## K Modification analysis: feasibility, objection, and equivalent access

Editing and analysis — Barrier/nexus: added language reconstruction and correction work described by Hanson and illustrated by E03/E08. Proposed change: a supported transformation workflow with contextual review, explicit correction persistence, and separately flagged factual disagreement. Existing capability evidence: the product’s observed ability to rewrite, including the attempted repair in E03; availability of a special behavioral override is UNRESOLVED. Objection: content policy, misuse, or product redesign. Relevant evidence: actual requested transformations and whether a narrower allowed operation succeeds. Alternative: supported transcription plus controlled editing, tested for equivalent cognitive workload. No guarantee of perfect meaning preservation is asserted.

Continuity — Barrier/nexus: lost task state requires repeated organization and restatement. Proposed change: export and selected-context restoration with implementation assistance. Existing capabilities vary by surface; private-mode history may be intentionally unavailable. Objection: retention conflicts and integration burden. Relevant evidence: actual restoration steps, accessibility of the client, setup/recurring effort, interrupted-task completion, and added disclosures. Alternative: accessible local history or a supported privacy-preserving workspace. Mere API availability does not establish equivalence.

Privacy — Barrier/nexus: the detailed context used for cognitive support makes repeated redaction/reconstruction burdensome and may permit sensitive inference. Proposed change: training exclusion, compatible retention/access controls, and user-controlled continuity. Existing candidates: OpenAI project ZDR/PSP; Google model-and-feature-specific Cloud controls; Anthropic eligible ZDR or available safeguards; Meta available Incognito with search off; Grok team ZDR; Apple on-device/PCC or explicitly governed extensions. Objection: safety monitoring, mandatory logs, plan boundaries, operating costs, or necessary state. Relevant evidence: exact settings, model eligibility, storage lifecycle, human access, retained signals, and service degradation. Alternative: a supported configuration meeting the same functions, including accessible local processing where feasible. Availability elsewhere is feasibility evidence, not a conclusive entitlement.

Administrative access — Barrier/nexus: repeated narrative reconstruction and redisclosure across support teams. Proposed change: one coordinating human contact, written determinations, and a secure limited-evidence channel. Existing evidence: published support/accessibility routes, not a confirmed dedicated adjudication office. Objection: ordinary support structure or resource burden. Alternative: a shared case record or authorized representative workflow that avoids repeated intake. The requested fourteen-day response is a proposed timetable; the cited ADA provisions do not establish that fixed deadline.

Fundamental alteration and cost — The statutory modification exception is fundamental alteration. The auxiliary-aid provision also contains undue burden; do not treat those as identical tests. Existing controls can make an individualized change plausible, but proof must address actual technical dependencies, safety functions, business operations, recurring burden, and effectiveness of alternatives. Approval requirements, customer-cloud costs, and inaccessible setup work remain part of that assessment. [L1–L4, L8–L9]

## L Do not use after this audit

Do not identify trainees or include identifying biography/source links. The retained statement is that Hanson trained two individuals who subsequently became physicians, classified as his firsthand account. Do not suggest his competence is something these letters must earn or prove to establish disability rights.

Do not cite McNeil under a different insurer’s name; the case is McNeil v. Time Insurance Co. Do not omit its adverse product-content reasoning or Magee’s physical-place reasoning. Do not state that Martin eliminated those questions or already resolved AI privacy controls.

Do not call the Dillon review TBI-only, give the Wang taxonomy an invented title, or classify the published Staab conference paper as merely unreviewed. Perry is the correct lead author of the neurodevelopmental review; the title and population limits, rather than the author attribution, required correction.

Do not call announced Anthropic safeguards universally available, customer-owned storage retention-free, OpenAI PSP a ChatGPT setting, or Meta web search off by default. Do not infer consumer protection from API documentation. Do not assume every high-capability model can operate without safety retention.

Do not infer malicious intent, a psychiatric diagnosis, a secret institutional unit, a confirmed data breach, or illegal retaliation from a model’s narrative. Claims concerning unrelated outages or a laboratory-company breach have no verified evidentiary foundation in this accommodation packet and are not used.
'''
t+='\\n## Source register\\n\\n'+ '\\n'.join(f'- [{k}: {v[\"title\"]}]({v[\"url\"]}) — accessed {v[\"accessed\"]}; publication/update: {v[\"publication_or_update\"]}.' for k,v in reg.items())+'\\n'
p.write_text(t)

# Renderable DOCX from the corrected authoritative memorandum.
def add_inline(p,txt):
 for part in re.split(r'(\\[["'^'"\\]]+\\]\\(https?://["'^'"\\s)]+\\))',txt):
  m=re.fullmatch(r'\\[(["'^'"\\]]+)\\]\\((https?://["'^'"\\s)]+)\\)',part)
  if m:ns['hyperlink'](p,m.group(1),m.group(2))
  else:p.add_run(part)
d=Document();sec=d.sections[0];sec.top_margin=sec.bottom_margin=Inches(.7);sec.left_margin=sec.right_margin=Inches(.8)
for st,size in [('Normal',10.5),('Title',18),('Heading 1',13)]:
 s=d.styles[st];s.font.name='Calibri';s.font.size=Pt(size);s.font.color.rgb=RGBColor(0,0,0);s.paragraph_format.space_after=Pt(6);s.paragraph_format.line_spacing=1.03
for block in t.split('\\n\\n'):
 if block.startswith('# '):d.add_paragraph(block[2:],style='Title')
 elif block.startswith('## '):d.add_paragraph(block[3:],style='Heading 1')
 elif block.startswith('- '):
  for line in block.splitlines():add_inline(d.add_paragraph(style='List Bullet'),line.removeprefix('- '))
